# Relaxed U: two dense regenerative flow laws with genuine star sources

JOINT UNVERIFIED. One direct analytical invocation, allocation
`52911a3e705b3954`, 2026-10-08. Original minimax target OPEN.

## 1. Outcome and scope

The relaxed coefficient interface was derived independently from the original
normal rows. Its source budget is exactly `1/8`. The complete matrix retains
the physical terminal gradient, the auxiliary gradient, all physical pairs,
and auxiliary-to-physical rows. Neither common-gradient contact nor
absorption nor a terminal-zero quotient is assumed.

Exactly two explicit laws were tested within one mechanism: every nonstar
source routes according to one common destination distribution, with a
circulating flow and two genuine star-source rows. In both laws, the star
sends `1/16` to the physical terminal and `1/16` to the auxiliary point.
Both laws have strictly positive auxiliary-to-physical coefficients and a
strictly positive common-gradient value. Every original coefficient is
specified below and is nonnegative.

- Law 1 assigns half the destination probability to the physical canonical
  mass profile. Its literal `g_3` diagonal is strictly negative on every
  actual `H_m`, for every enclosing `m>=1536`.
- Law 2 reduces the physical destination probability according to the
  largest step. All coordinate diagonals then become positive. Nevertheless,
  its full two-coordinate principal block consisting of the largest-step
  gradient and the terminal gradient has strictly negative determinant.
  This obstruction holds for every admissible amount of circulating flow,
  including amounts larger than the explicit law uses.

These are analytical exclusions of the stated coefficient laws. The formal
negative directions are not asserted to be projection histories. No genuine
GD counterexample, full-class upper bound, or asymptotic separation was
obtained. The all-depth construction is BLOCKED at these two laws.

## 2. Governing inputs and normalization

The current `OPERATOR.md`, original project `PROBLEM.md`, and complete TASK
were read. The operator's current explicit continuation and live-Goal
confirmation supersede the historical pause notes.

Accepted `1e0bf3f41457d8e4`, Parts A--C, supplies finite-word
projection-simplex universality, the entire polar minimum set, and the
actual same-enclosing-parameter H3 law. Accepted `931b9653ce0f7201` supplies
the benchmark ratio. Their relevant statements were read directly; the
actual H3 identity was also read. They do not supply the all-depth bound.

The relaxed-interface lead and the prior projection-flow report were used
as unverified leads/conventions. The original equations below are derived
anew. The complete-graph H3/H4 numerical report was read only as recorded
finite evidence, without loading its arrays or treating its candidates as
premises. The prior joint-reserve report's two failed laws and their scope
were read: neither its absorption-product gluing nor its moment-dual
destination rule is retried here. The present common-destination routing
is global on the original word; it has no inherited child multiplier
array, child reserve, or child moment-matching equation.

Fix one enclosing integer `m>=1536` and use throughout all children

\[
 \varepsilon=\frac1{4m},\quad \beta=1-\varepsilon,\quad
 c=(1+\sqrt2)(1+\varepsilon),\quad a=\beta/c,
 \quad H_0=(),\quad H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+ac^j,H_j).
\]

For the actual full word let `n=2^m-1`, `S=sum H_m`, and `M=1+2S`.
The target of this invocation is

\[
 U_1(T_{H_m}x)\le\alpha U_M(x),\qquad
 \alpha=17/16,\quad \eta=\alpha-1=1/16.
\tag{2.1}
\]

Here `U_d(x)=max_{g in D}(2<x,g>-d||g||^2)` for every nonempty compact
convex body `D` containing zero, in every finite dimension. Physical GD
uses `P_D` and the original steps. The original error normalization remains
`W=2R`; the certificate polynomial below is unrelated to that error `R`.

## 3. Derivation of the full relaxed interface

The following derivation applies to any finite nonnegative word, with
`S>0`. Define physical labels `0,...,n`, auxiliary label `p`, and star `*`.
Write

\[
 T_i=\sum_{k<i}h_k,\quad t_i=1+T_i,\quad t_p=M,
 \quad G_i=g_i=P_D(x_i),\quad G_p=p=P_D(x_0/M),
\]

\[
 B_iG=g_i+\sum_{k<i}h_kg_k,\qquad B_pG=Mp,
 \qquad X_*=G_*=0.
\tag{3.1}
\]

For physical destinations put `a_j=1`; for destination `p` put `a_p=1/M`.
Then `X_j-G_j=a_j(x_0-B_jG)`. The original projection normals are

\[
 Q_{ij}=\langle X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j\rangle\le0.
\tag{3.2}
\]

For nonnegative original multipliers `lambda_ij`, the sufficient
certificate is

\[
 R_\alpha=J_\alpha+\sum_{i,j}\lambda_{ij}Q_{ij}\succeq0,
 \qquad J_\alpha=\alpha U_M(x_0)-U_1(x_n),
\tag{3.3}
\]

where the actual maximizer expressions replace both U values before
forming a polynomial in the formal coordinates. On an actual history,
`J_alpha>=R_alpha`; hence a globally PSD polynomial proves (2.1).
No completeness or dual-attainment assertion about this certificate cone
is used.

Rows with destination `*`, and diagonal rows, are identically zero and
are assigned coefficient zero. For every other row set
`mu_ij=a_j lambda_ij`. Let `I={0,...,n,p}`,
`s_j=mu_{*,j}`, and define `out_i` and `in_i` using only the internal
edges on I, with the self edges omitted. Expanding the objective gives

\[
 J_\alpha=2\langle x_0,\alpha p-g_n\rangle
  +2\Big\langle g_n,\sum_{k<n}h_kg_k\Big\rangle
  +\|g_n\|^2-\alpha M\|p\|^2.
\tag{3.4}
\]

There is no squared x0 term. Global PSD therefore forces its entire
linear coefficient to vanish. Coordinate by coordinate this is exactly

\[
 \boxed{\operatorname{out}_i-\operatorname{in}_i-s_i
       =2\mathbf1_{i=n}-2\alpha\mathbf1_{i=p},\qquad i\in I.}
\tag{3.5}
\]

Summation gives

\[
 \boxed{\sum_{j\in I}s_j=2(\alpha-1)=1/8.}
\tag{3.6}
\]

Thus full divergence, including the star, is the proposed relaxed
divergence. This derivation does not force any of the `s_j` or any
auxiliary outgoing coefficient to vanish.

After (3.5), the complete polynomial is

\[
 \boxed{R_\alpha=
 2\Big\langle g_n,\sum_{k<n}h_kg_k\Big\rangle+\|g_n\|^2
 -\alpha M\|p\|^2
 +\sum_{i,j\in I}\mu_{ij}\langle B_jG,G_j-G_i\rangle
 +\sum_{j\in I}s_j\langle B_jG,G_j\rangle.}
\tag{3.7}
\]

If `b_j` is the coordinate column of `B_j`, `H=sum h_i e_i`, and
`sym(u,v)=(uv^T+vu^T)/2`, its full symmetric matrix is

\[
 P=2\operatorname{sym}(e_n,H)+e_ne_n^T-\alpha Me_pe_p^T
   +\sum_{i,j\in I}\mu_{ij}\operatorname{sym}(b_j,e_j-e_i)
   +\sum_{j\in I}s_j\operatorname{sym}(b_j,e_j).
\tag{3.8}
\]

For additional explicit coefficient checks, put `h_n=0`. The diagonal
and auxiliary cross coefficients are

\[
 P_{ii}=\operatorname{in}_i+s_i-h_i\sum_{j=i+1}^{n}\mu_{ij}
       \quad(i<n),\qquad P_{nn}=1+\operatorname{in}_n+s_n,
\tag{3.9}
\]

\[
 P_{pp}=M(\alpha+\operatorname{out}_p),\qquad
 2P_{ip}=-M\mu_{ip}-\mu_{pi}
          -h_i\sum_{j=i+1}^{n}\mu_{pj}\quad(0\le i\le n).
\tag{3.10}
\]

For `0<=i<j<=n`, the physical cross coefficient is

\[
 \begin{split}
 2P_{ij}={}&2h_i\mathbf1_{j=n}+h_i(\operatorname{in}_j+s_j)
 -\mu_{ji}-\mu_{ij}\\
 &-h_j\sum_{\ell=j+1}^{n}\mu_{i\ell}
 -h_i\sum_{\ell=i+1}^{n}\mu_{j\ell}.
 \end{split}
\tag{3.11}
\]

Equations (3.8)--(3.11) retain every original gradient coordinate. In
particular, (3.10) follows from the p balance in (3.5), not from absorption.

At a common nonstar gradient `G_i=v`, internal row terms vanish and

\[
 R_\alpha(v)=\left[-\eta M+\sum_j s_jt_j\right]\|v\|^2.
\tag{3.12}
\]

PSD requires this scalar to be nonnegative. It need not be zero, so there
is no general first-variation contact equation or common-translation
invariance.

One valid auxiliary reduction, if desired, follows directly from (3.10).
Set `A_p=M(alpha+out_p)>0` and

\[
 k_i=M\mu_{ip}+\mu_{pi}+h_i\sum_{j>i}\mu_{pj},\qquad
 z=\frac{\sum_{i=0}^{n}k_i g_i}{2A_p}.
\]

Then `R_alpha=A_p||p-z||^2+K(G_0,...,G_n)` by exact completion of
the p square. Moreover `k_i>=0` and

\[
 2A_p-\sum_i k_i=M s_p+\sum_{j=0}^{n}\mu_{pj}(M-t_j)\ge0,
\tag{3.13}
\]

so z is a subprobability mixture of the physical gradients, with its
remaining weight at zero. This is not p absorption and does not permit
setting `g_n=0` by a quotient. The exclusions below test literal principal
blocks of (3.8), without using this reduction.

## 4. One mechanism: dense regenerative routing

Define the physical probability profile

\[
 \pi_i=h_i/M\ (i<n),\qquad \pi_n=(S+1)/M,
 \qquad \sum_{i=0}^{n}\pi_i=1.
\tag{4.1}
\]

Choose a physical destination fraction `0<theta<1`, and set

\[
 q_i=\theta\pi_i\ (i\le n),\quad q_p=1-\theta,
 \quad \kappa=2+\eta=33/16,
 \quad L=\frac{2\kappa}{q_p}.
\tag{4.2}
\]

From (4.2) through Section 6, the letter L denotes a routing intensity.
The objective's smoothness parameter is normalized to one throughout
these sections; no physical step is rescaled by the routing intensity.

Define intensities

\[
 r_i=Lq_i+\kappa\mathbf1_{i=n}-\kappa\mathbf1_{i=p}.
\tag{4.3}
\]

Every original coefficient of the two laws is the following:

\[
 \begin{array}{ll}
 \mu_{ij}=r_iq_j,&i,j\in I,\ i\ne j,\\
 s_n=\eta,\quad s_p=\eta,&s_i=0\ (i<n),\\
 \lambda_{ij}=\mu_{ij},&j\text{ physical},\\
 \lambda_{i,p}=M\mu_{i,p},&i\ne p,\\
 \lambda_{*,n}=\eta,\quad\lambda_{*,p}=M\eta,&
 \lambda_{*,i}=0\ (i<n).
 \end{array}
\tag{4.4}
\]

Self rows and destination-star rows are zero. Thus all unspecified
coefficients are also zero. The distinction between a routing intensity
and actual outgoing mass matters: `out_i=r_i(1-q_i)`, not `r_i`.

Because `sum q_i=1`, `sum r_i=L`, and `r_p=kappa>0`, all coefficients
are nonnegative and every p-to-physical row is strictly positive for the
actual strictly positive word. Directly,

\[
 \operatorname{out}_i-\operatorname{in}_i
 =r_i-Lq_i=\kappa\mathbf1_{i=n}-\kappa\mathbf1_{i=p}.
\tag{4.5}
\]

Subtracting the two star inputs gives divergence two at n, minus
`2 alpha` at p, and zero elsewhere. Hence the complete x0 cancellation
holds exactly. The star uses its entire `1/8` source budget.

Its common-gradient value is

\[
 R_\alpha(v)=\eta t_n\|v\|^2>0\qquad(v\ne0).
\tag{4.6}
\]

These laws therefore do not secretly reimpose the exact-U null condition.
Both physical-to-auxiliary and auxiliary-to-physical rows are present.

The two choices of theta below are the only laws tested. A useful robustness
observation is that (4.3)--(4.5) remain valid for any routing intensity
`L>=kappa/q_p`; the explicit laws use `2 kappa/q_p`. When analyzing the
second law's obstruction, allowing the larger interval of intensities is
an exclusion of possible repairs, not a third law.

## 5. Law 1: balanced destinations

Set `theta=1/2`, so `L=4 kappa=33/4`. This specifies every coefficient
of Law 1 via (4.4).

For a physical preterminal index i, let
`Q_i^+=sum_{j=i+1}^n q_j`. Formula (3.9) gives the exact literal diagonal

\[
 P_{ii}=Lq_i(1-q_i-h_iQ_i^+),\qquad
 q_i+h_iQ_i^+=\frac{\theta h_i(M+1-T_{i+1})}{M}.
\tag{5.1}
\]

No other coordinate has been eliminated. Set just `g_i=v`, with all other
gradients, including `p` and `g_n`, zero; (5.1) is the entire polynomial
on that formal assignment.

For the same-enclosing-m H3 prefix, write

\[
 C=1+S_2=4+2a+\beta,\qquad k=ac^2=\beta c,
 \qquad h_3=1+k,\quad T_4=C+k.
\tag{5.2}
\]

Its mass parameter is `M_3=4C+2k-1`. At `M=M_3`, strict negativity in
(5.1) is equivalent to

\[
 (1+k)(3C+k)>2(4C+2k-1).
\tag{5.3}
\]

The difference between the two sides is exactly

\[
 C(3k-5)+(k-1)(k-2)>0.
\tag{5.4}
\]

For its sign, `epsilon<1/10` and `1+sqrt(2)>12/5` give
`k=(1+sqrt(2))(1-epsilon^2)>297/125>7/3`. Both terms in
(5.4) are therefore strictly positive.

The original complete word H_m contains this identical H3 prefix and has
`M>=M_3`. Since `T_4>1`, the factor
`(M+1-T_4)/M=1-(T_4-1)/M` increases with M. Thus the same original
diagonal remains strictly negative for the complete word, for every
enclosing `m>=1536`. This is a full-space obstruction to Law 1, at one
specified original coordinate; no quotient or numerical calibration is
used.

## 6. Law 2: destinations damped by the largest step

Law 1 spends too much destination mass after a long step. For the second
and final law, let

\[
 d=\max_{i<n}h_i=1+ac^{m-1},\qquad
 \theta=\frac1{2d},\quad q_p=1-\frac1{2d},\quad
 L=\frac{2\kappa}{q_p}.
\tag{6.1}
\]

Again (4.4) specifies every coefficient. For the actual H_m, `d>3`.
The proof below works for every positive word having a step `d>=3` and
using this same profile and source split.

This adjustment repairs all coordinate diagonals. Indeed, for `i<n`,

\[
 q_i+h_iQ_i^+\le\theta+d\theta
 =\theta+\frac12\le\frac23,
\]

so `P_ii>=L q_i/3>0`. Also

\[
 P_{nn}=\alpha+(L-\kappa)q_n-Lq_n^2\ge\alpha>0,
\tag{6.2}
\]

because `L(1-q_n)>=Lq_p>=kappa`. Formula (3.10) gives a positive p
diagonal. These statements continue to hold if the routing intensity is
any `L>=kappa/q_p`.

Nevertheless the complete matrix fails on a two-coordinate original
principal block. Let i be an index carrying the largest step d, keep only
`g_i` and `g_n` free, and set every other coordinate, including p, zero.
Denote the two diagonal coefficients by `D_i=P_ii`, `D_n=P_nn`, and the
coefficient of `<g_i,g_n>` by C. This use of zero coordinates is a
particular formal test assignment, not an asserted quotient identity.

Put `q_+=sum_{j=i+1}^n q_j` and `y=Lq_n>0`. The exact original-row
formulas (3.9) and (3.11) give

\[
 \begin{split}
 D_i&=Lq_i(1-q_i-dq_+),\\
 D_n&=\alpha+(L-\kappa)q_n-Lq_n^2,\\
 C&=d(Lq_n+\kappa)(1-q_+)-(2Lq_n+\kappa)q_i.
 \end{split}
\tag{6.3}
\]

These formulas include the nonzero star-to-terminal row. In particular,
omitting it would incorrectly replace alpha in D_n and kappa in C.

Now

\[
 q_i=\frac1{2M},\qquad
 q_n=\frac{M+1}{4dM},\qquad q_+\le\frac1{2d}.
\tag{6.4}
\]

Since `S>=d`, we have `M>=2d+1`. Using (6.3)--(6.4),

\[
 D_iD_n\le\frac{L}{2M}(\alpha+y)
 =\frac{2d}{M+1}y(\alpha+y)<y(\alpha+y).
\tag{6.5}
\]

The cross coefficient has the lower bound

\[
 \begin{split}
 C&\ge\left(d-\frac12-\frac1M\right)y
       +\kappa\left(d-\frac12-\frac1{2M}\right)\\
  &\ge(d-1)(y+\kappa)>0.
 \end{split}
\tag{6.6}
\]

Because `d>=3`, (6.6) implies `C^2/4>=(y+kappa)^2`. But

\[
 (y+\kappa)^2-y(y+\alpha)
 =(2\kappa-\alpha)y+\kappa^2>0.
\tag{6.7}
\]

Consequently the actual principal determinant is strictly negative:

\[
 \boxed{D_iD_n-C^2/4<0.}
\tag{6.8}
\]

An explicit full formal negative direction is `g_i=v`,
`g_n=-C v/(2D_n)`, with every other coordinate zero. Its polynomial
value is `(D_i-C^2/(4D_n))||v||^2<0`. This keeps the terminal gradient
nonzero and tests the full unreduced polynomial. It is not asserted to
satisfy the projection normals.

The derivation of (6.8) holds for every `L>=kappa/q_p`, not only the
particular intensity in (6.1). Hence increasing the circular traffic,
including the auxiliary return flow, cannot repair this law. It fails
despite the correct source budget, exact divergence, nonnegative original
coefficients, positive common-gradient value, and positive coordinate
diagonals.

## 7. Exact full-W implication of the still-missing U bound

For completeness, the sufficient target (2.1) must use the entire polar
minimum set, not an origin-radius surrogate. For any allowed D, set

\[
 C_D=\{z:\langle z,g\rangle\le0\text{ for all }g\in D\}.
\]

This is exactly `argmin F_D`, with minimum zero. Membership makes every
candidate in F_D nonpositive, while `g=0` attains zero. Conversely, a
positive `<z,g>` produces a positive value using a sufficiently small
multiple of g in D. The set is closed, convex and nonempty.

For every selected minimum `z in C_D`, every g in D, and M>0,

\[
 2\langle x,g\rangle-M\|g\|^2
 \le 2\langle x-z,g\rangle-M\|g\|^2
 \le\frac{\|x-z\|^2}{M}.
\]

Thus `U_M(x)<=dist(x,C_D)^2/M<=||x-z||^2/M`. Since `U_1=2F_D`, an
all-body proof of (2.1) for each sufficiently large m would imply

\[
 2F_D(T_{H_m}x)\le\frac{17}{16M}\|x-z\|^2
\]

for every selected minimum and start. Starts in the minimum set are
stationary and have zero value. Accepted finite-word universality then
gives, for each fixed m separately,

\[
 \mathcal W_{2^m-1}(H_m)\le\frac{17}{16M}.
\tag{7.1}
\]

There is no horizon/supremum interchange. Multiplication of F_D by the
advertised smoothness and use of steps h/L preserves the original
normalization. Accepted `B_(2^m-1)/M<47/50` would then yield

\[
 \frac{\mathcal W_{2^m-1}^\star}{U_{2^m-1}^{\rm ZJ}}
 \le B_{2^m-1}\mathcal W_{2^m-1}(H_m)<\frac{799}{800}.
\]

It is enough that (7.1) hold eventually along these horizons to deduce
`liminf W_n^*/U_n^ZJ<=799/800<1`. The missing hypothesis remains the
uniform all-body, all-dimension, all-start upper bound (2.1). Neither
coefficient exclusion proves or disproves it.

## 8. Remaining gap and resource closeout

The exclusions concern the explicitly specified global common-destination
routing profile, at its two prescribed physical mass fractions and its
fixed equal star split between n and p. They do not exclude general dense
original-row flows, other destination profiles, other star allocations,
additional auxiliary projections, or the relaxed U inequality itself.
The second exclusion specifically identifies a mixed original-gradient
obstruction that survives all circulating-flow increases; checking only
diagonals would have missed it.

No all-depth PSD law was constructed. Replacing the remaining construction
by feasibility of (3.8) would be an equivalent certificate search, not a
solution; this invocation makes no such progress claim. A subsequent
authorized route would need a new coefficient mechanism or independent
mathematical information, rather than another circulation intensity in
these two laws.

Exactly one analytical invocation and exactly two laws were used. There
were zero mathematical programs, arithmetic/symbolic/trajectory/recurrence

# R6 handoff

UNVERIFIED analytical draft. Original target OPEN. No complete affirmative or negative candidate.

One invocation, two substantive lines, mathematical PROGRAM0, arrays/solvers0, formal0, no additional agent/model. Only the two requested R6 output files were written. Parent owns ledger and closeout.

## Decisive result

Common-normal translation is valid, but its exact changes are

\[
H\mapsto H+t,\quad M\mapsto M+t,\quad F_n\mapsto F_n+t,\quad
R^2\mapsto R^2+2t\langle x_0,e\rangle+t^2\|e\|^2.
\]

If the minimum-norm affine foot belongs to the retained nonzero gradient hull, orthogonal projection off that common component produces another actual static projection-gradient function with the same complete physical word. With tangent radius \(D\), tangent terminal value \(V=2F_T\), common gradient component \(c\), and initial normal coordinate \(y\), the full error is

\[
\frac{V+2cy-(2S+1)c^2}{D+y^2}
\le\max\{V/D,\ 1/(2S+1)\}.
\]

This is an exact, whole-word domination statement. The tangent history is explicitly realized, not a formal Gram direction.

For the R5 orthogonal retained-height construction, the foot condition holds and every legal translation leaves the initial point in the gradient cone. Therefore 0 is genuinely the closest minimizer; no polar-cone improvement was omitted. Its tangent generators form a centered simplex after scaling.

The second line explicitly restores a common normal to the R5 centered simplex with a shared gradient component and negative cross correlations. Every old and minimum row is checked. Its initial point is again in the gradient cone, and the same domination applies for every legal lift and positive replenishment parameter. Optimizing or repeating this repair cannot outperform its actual centered child or the Huber witness.

The foot condition is also automatic for linearly independent generators with nonpositive cross inner products, by positivity of the solution of their Gram system. This broader observation does not itself prove that 0 is closest for arbitrary histories; the report keeps that distinction explicit.

## Baseline-surviving word check

The parent's R6_QUADRATIC_BASELINE_BOUND_20261009.md was read. Its exact multiplicities, mass inductions, and three frequency cases were checked manually and support
\(D_m\sup_\lambda\lambda p_m(\lambda)^2\le9/16\).
Conditional on the parent-reported accepted comparison \(B/D_m<47/50\), the inherited-parameter hierarchy survives the complete Huber-plus-scalar-quadratic pool. This invocation did not independently re-read that accepted fact from the fact service.

On those words, a replenished witness can succeed only if its centered tangent history already supplies the missing target-level lower bound. No upper bound on all such tangent histories was obtained. Do not call this a full-family failure or a negative resolution.

## Remaining original-target work

There is still no proof selecting centered or otherwise compatible histories for every surviving word with a uniform \((1-o(1))/B_n\) payoff. The proposed common-normal replenishment does not fill that gap. Do not renew unchanged common shifts as though their height increase were free.

The R6 allocation is complete and stops here. No formal submission or automatic renewal is requested.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002833 -->
# R6: exact cost of common-normal height replenishment

2026-10-09. UNVERIFIED analytical draft. Original target OPEN.

This invocation used two substantive lines: translating an actual common-normal history, and repairing the loss with negatively correlated gradients that retain a common normal. The full original lower bound was not obtained. The decisive result of this attempt is an exact, whole-trajectory domination statement for the proposed replenishment mechanism, with its closest-minimizer scope made explicit.

One invocation; mathematical PROGRAM0, arrays/solvers0, formal0, no additional model or agent. All constructions and estimates below are manual analytical drafts, not accepted project facts.

## 1. Target and actual baseline witnesses

After setting \(L=1\), let \(h\in[0,\infty)^n\) be the entire prescribed word, \(S=\sum_i h_i\), and

\[
x_{i+1}=x_i-h_i\nabla f(x_i),\qquad
W_n(h)=\sup\frac{2(f(x_n)-f_\star)}{\|x_0-x_\star\|^2}.
\]

The supremum has the original full finite-dimensional smooth-convex scope, with an attained minimum and the original last iterate and radius. Let \(C_0=B_0=1\) and, for every integer split \(k+l=n-1\),

\[
C_n=\max\frac{C_k+C_l+\sqrt{C_k^2+6C_kC_l+C_l^2}}2,
\]

\[
B_n=\max\left(2C_k+\frac{B_l}2+
\frac{\sqrt{B_l^2+8C_kB_l}}2\right).
\]

The missing original conclusion is

\[
\forall\epsilon>0\ \exists N_\epsilon\
\forall n\ge N_\epsilon\ \forall h\in[0,\infty)^n:
\quad W_n(h)\ge(1-\epsilon)/B_n,
\]

with \(N_\epsilon\) independent of \(h\).

The complete global Huber and scalar-quadratic pool is retained:

\[
W_n(h)\ge \frac1{2S+1},\qquad
W_n(h)\ge\sup_{0\le\lambda\le1}
\lambda\prod_i(1-h_i\lambda)^2.
\tag{1}
\]

No completeness of this pool is assumed. Nor is Zhang--Jiang's sufficient concatenation theorem used as a converse.

## 2. Line 1: translating a fixed common-normal history

### 2.1 Function, geometry and exact translation law

Let \(K=\operatorname{conv}(0,g_1,\ldots,g_m)\), where the nonzero retained generators include all queried gradients. Use the single static function

\[
F_K(x)=\max_{g\in K}
\{\langle x,g\rangle-\tfrac12\|g\|^2\}.
\tag{2}
\]

It is convex and 1-smooth, \(\nabla F_K=P_K\), and \(F_K(0)=0=\min F_K\). Its normal test at a retained jet is

\[
\langle x_i-g_i,g-g_i\rangle\le0\quad(g\in K).
\tag{3}
\]

A history with positive terminal objective cannot have encountered gradient 0 earlier, so useful histories may be treated with nonzero queried gradients throughout.

Suppose an actual vector \(e\) satisfies

\[
\langle e,g_j\rangle=1\qquad(1\le j\le m).
\tag{4}
\]

Define actual quantities

\[
H=\min_i\langle x_i-g_i,g_i\rangle,\quad
M=\max_j\langle x_0,g_j\rangle,\quad
R^2=\|x_0\|^2,\quad A=\langle x_0,e\rangle,\quad E=\|e\|^2.
\]

For \(t\ge-H\), translate the entire trajectory to \(x_i+t e\), keeping the same body \(K\) and gradients. All nonzero-generator differences in (3) are unchanged. The rows against 0 become exactly \(H_i+t\ge0\). The GD recursion also remains exact, because the same translation is made at every time.

The values and complete boundary data are therefore

\[
F_K(x_i+t e)=F_K(x_i)+t,\quad
H(t)=H+t,\quad M(t)=M+t,
\]

\[
R(t)^2=R^2+2At+Et^2.
\tag{5}
\]

Thus height replenishment is legal, but its original radius is the quadratic expression in (5), not \(R^2\). To raise \(H/M\) to a specified \(\eta_\star\in(H/M,1)\) requires

\[
t=\frac{\eta_\star M-H}{1-\eta_\star}>0.
\tag{6}
\]

For a proposed target denominator \(B\), the exact selected-origin test is

\[
2B(F_K(x_n)+t)-R(t)^2
=2BF_K(x_n)-R^2+2(B-A)t-Et^2.
\tag{7}
\]

All quantities in (5)--(7) belong to this actual history. No favorable value of \(A\) or \(E\) is an independent resource.

For the R5 orthogonal prefix-to-child operation, a child's common normal is inherited explicitly. If the added prefix gradient is \((a,0)\) and the child generators are \((0,b_j)\), then

\[
e_{\rm new}=(1/a,e_B),\qquad
E_{\rm new}=1/a^2+E_B,\qquad
A_{\rm new}=C+v+A_B.
\tag{8}
\]

These identities show exactly where repeated replenishment would pay its additional radius.

### 2.2 The complete minimum cone and the closest-origin condition

For (2),

\[
\arg\min F_K=K^\circ
=\{z:\langle z,g_j\rangle\le0\ \forall j\}.
\tag{9}
\]

The origin is a closest minimizer to \(x_0\) if and only if \(x_0\in\operatorname{cone}K\). Indeed, 0 is the closest point in the polar cone precisely when \(\langle x_0,z\rangle\le0\) for every \(z\in K^\circ\); otherwise a sufficiently small positive multiple of such a \(z\) reduces the distance. The polar of this closed polyhedral polar cone is \(\operatorname{cone}K\).

This condition holds for the R5 orthogonal retained-height histories and every legal common-normal translation of them. Write their generators as \(a_j e_j\), \(a_j>0\), and their initial coordinate supports as \(P_j=a_j(x_0)_j\). At the first query using generator \(j\), its coordinate has not yet moved, so the height there is \(P_j-a_j^2\). Therefore

\[
H\le P_j-a_j^2,\qquad t\ge-H
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad P_j+t\ge a_j^2>0.
\]

Since the common normal is \((1/a_j)_j\), the translated initial point still lies in the positive coordinate cone. Thus 0 really is the closest minimizer, including after the replenishment. There is no omitted improvement from selecting a different minimum in this family.

For a general common-normal hull, (4) alone does not imply the closest-origin condition. An upper bound using the origin radius must not be promoted to an upper bound using the closest minimum without this separate check.

### 2.3 Removing the common component gives another actual whole-word history

Take the minimum-norm solution of (4), and suppose that its affine perpendicular foot lies in the retained nonzero hull:

\[
p=\frac e{\|e\|^2}\in
\operatorname{conv}(g_1,\ldots,g_m).
\tag{10}
\]

Put

\[
\nu=e/\|e\|,\qquad c=1/\|e\|,\qquad
g_j=w_j+c\nu,\qquad w_j\perp\nu.
\]

By (10), \(0\in K_T:=\operatorname{conv}(w_1,\ldots,w_m)\). For the physical points write
\(x_i=y_i+z_i\nu\). The GD recursion gives, at every actual time,

\[
y_{i+1}=y_i-h_iw_i,\qquad
z_i=z_0-c\sum_{r<i}h_r.
\tag{11}
\]

The projected history is not a formal Gram direction: it is realized by the explicit static function

\[
F_T(y)=\max_{w\in K_T}
\{\langle y,w\rangle-\tfrac12\|w\|^2\}.
\tag{12}
\]

Indeed every old nonzero-generator row becomes

\[
\langle y_i-w_i,w_j-w_i\rangle
=\langle x_i-g_i,g_j-g_i\rangle\le0.
\]

Since 0 is a convex combination of the \(w_j\), its row follows as well. Hence \(\nabla F_T(y_i)=w_i\), all physical steps are retained, and 0 is an attained minimum of (12).

Let

\[
D=\|y_0\|^2,\qquad V=2F_T(y_n),\qquad Q=2S+1.
\]

The complete original value and radius split exactly:

\[
2F_K(x_n)=V+2cz_0-Qc^2,\qquad
\|x_0\|^2=D+z_0^2.
\tag{13}
\]

For every real \(z_0\),

\[
2cz_0-Qc^2
\le \frac{z_0^2}{Q},
\tag{14}
\]

because \((z_0-Qc)^2\ge0\). If \(D>0\), (13)--(14) imply the pointwise domination

\[
\boxed{\quad
\frac{2F_K(x_n)}{\|x_0\|^2}
\le
\max\left\{\frac{2F_T(y_n)}{\|y_0\|^2},
\frac1{2S+1}\right\}.
\quad}
\tag{15}
\]

If \(D=0\), the tangent history starts at its minimum and remains there, so \(V=0\); the Huber term alone bounds (13). This covers that degeneracy.

A common-normal translation changes only \(z_0\) in (13). Thus (15) includes every legal translation, every horizon and every nonnegative word, with no asymptotic loss calculation or per-level approximation. If the closest-origin condition of Section 2.2 holds, it also bounds the error normalized by the closest valid minimizer.

### 2.4 What the domination means for the actual orthogonal recursion

For the R5 orthogonal generators \(g_j=a_je_j\), the foot condition (10) is automatic:

\[
E=\sum_j a_j^{-2},\qquad
p=\sum_j\frac{a_j^{-2}}E\,g_j.
\]

Its tangent generators satisfy

\[
\langle w_i,w_j\rangle=-1/E\quad(i\ne j).
\]

After a common scaling, they are precisely a centered simplex of the form

\[
\sqrt{A_j}e_j-u,\qquad
A_j=Ea_j^2,\quad \sum_j A_j^{-1}=1.
\]

The initial tangent point and its complete GD chronology come from (11), not from freely chosen support data. Together with the closest-minimizer proof, (15) shows that the entire translated orthogonal retained-height construction is dominated, word by word, by an actual centered tangent history or the global Huber witness.

This is not a proof that the centered history has a small error. It can itself be the needed nonlinear adversary. The conclusion is that common-normal replenishment supplies no additional normalized contribution beyond those two actual witnesses. A proof still has to establish the required centered-history payoff on the hard words.

For this orthogonal family, the centered part also loses the proposed replenishment operation. If it is nontrivial, its strictly positive convex combination giving \(\sum_j\lambda_j w_j=0\), \(\sum_j\lambda_j=1\), prevents any vector from having inner product 1 with all its nonzero generators. Thus the claimed recursive repair cannot simply be repeated on that centered child with the same premise.

## 3. Line 2: retaining a common normal through negative correlations

### 3.1 An actual coupled lift of the R5 simplex

Use an actual R5 simplex history. Its nonzero generators \(w_j\) have pairwise inner product \(-1\), satisfy
\(\sum_j A_j^{-1}w_j=0\), and have the explicit initial point

\[
x_0=\sum_j (P_j/A_j)w_j,\qquad
\mu=\sum_j P_j/A_j.
\]

The R5 phase proof gives, at a query using vertex \(j\), a nonnegative phase score \(L_i\) with

\[
H_i:=\langle x_i-w_j,w_j\rangle
=L_i+s_i+1-\mu\ge0,\qquad L_i\ge0,
\tag{16}
\]

where \(s_i\) is the actual cumulative mass before the query. These are realized histories, including all old values and cross rows.

Let \(e_\perp\) be a new unit vector orthogonal to their span. For
\(0<\gamma\le1\) and \(t\ge0\), choose one fixed lifted body

\[
\widehat K=\operatorname{conv}
\bigl(0,\ w_j+\gamma e_\perp:\text{all retained }j\bigr)
\]

and the initial point

\[
\widehat x_0=x_0+
\left(\gamma\mu+\frac t\gamma\right)e_\perp.
\tag{17}
\]

Prescribe the lifted gradient \(w_j+\gamma e_\perp\) whenever the original history uses \(w_j\). The new coordinate evolves by subtracting \(\gamma\) times every physical step.

All nonzero-generator cross rows are unchanged. The row against 0 at query \(i\) is paid by the height

\[
\widehat H_i
=H_i+\gamma^2(\mu-s_i-1)+t
=(1-\gamma^2)H_i+\gamma^2L_i+t\ge0.
\tag{18}
\]

Thus (17)--(18) realize the entire lifted trajectory on one static smooth convex function. The lifted generators have cross inner products \(-1+\gamma^2\le0\) and have common normal \(e_\perp/\gamma\). Its norm is \(1/\gamma\); it cannot be held fixed when the lift is made small.

### 3.2 The minimum cone and full radius remain explicit

Let \(\lambda_j=1/A_j\). The coefficients

\[
\alpha_j=P_j/A_j+(t/\gamma^2)\lambda_j
\]

are nonnegative, and

\[
\sum_j\alpha_j(w_j+\gamma e_\perp)=\widehat x_0.
\]

Therefore \(\widehat x_0\in\operatorname{cone}\widehat K\), and 0 is a closest minimizer of the lifted function. This rules out a hidden polar-cone improvement in this repair.

Writing \(D=\|x_0\|^2\), \(V=2F_K(x_n)\), and
\(Y=\gamma\mu+t/\gamma\), its complete original radius and terminal value are

\[
\|\widehat x_0\|^2=D+Y^2,\qquad
2F_{\widehat K}(\widehat x_n)=V+2\gamma Y-(2S+1)\gamma^2.
\tag{19}
\]

The same exact inequality (14) now gives

\[
\frac{2F_{\widehat K}(\widehat x_n)}
{\operatorname{dist}(\widehat x_0,\arg\min F_{\widehat K})^2}
\le
\max\left\{\frac{2F_K(x_n)}{\|x_0\|^2},
\frac1{2S+1}\right\}.
\tag{20}
\]

This holds for every legal phase partition, every R5 simplex parameter choice, every \(0<\gamma\le1\), every \(t\ge0\), and the actual arbitrary nonnegative word underlying the history. Optimizing these lift and replenishment parameters cannot beat the existing centered child or Huber. This is a pointwise portfolio domination, not a failure observed on a baseline-covered Silver example.

### 3.3 Scope beyond this explicit lift

The foot condition behind this conclusion is not special to orthogonal generators. For linearly independent generators with positive-definite Gram matrix \(G\) and nonpositive off-diagonal entries, the solution of \(G\lambda=\mathbf1\) is strictly positive. One proof minimizes

\[
\tfrac12 a^TGa-\mathbf1^Ta.
\]

Replacing \(a\) by its componentwise absolute values cannot increase the quadratic term and strictly improves the linear term if a negative component is present. The minimizer is nonnegative. A zero component would make the corresponding row of \(G\lambda\) nonpositive, contradicting its value 1. Hence every component is positive.

Consequently the minimum-norm common-normal foot is a positive convex combination of these generators. The tangent construction of Section 2.3 applies whenever a common-normal history of this type is actually realized. This statement establishes the foot condition only; its closest-minimizer condition still requires the separate cone check. That check was proved for (17), not assumed for every possible correlated hull.

This repair therefore did not produce a new recursive source of normalized error. It returns the problem to the actual centered child, where a common normal is absent and the all-word history selection still needs a different proof.

## 4. Check on a genuine baseline-surviving hierarchy

The parent's auxiliary draft R6_QUADRATIC_BASELINE_BOUND_20261009.md was read and its scalar-polynomial argument checked manually. Its parameters are fixed throughout each enclosing-depth word:

\[
\epsilon=\frac1{4m},\quad \beta=1-\epsilon,\quad
c=(1+\sqrt2)(1+\epsilon),\quad a=\beta/c,
\]

\[
H_0=(),\qquad H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+ac^j,H_j).
\]

For \(m\ge4\), write \(S_j=\sum H_j\), \(D_m=1+2S_m\). The mass inductions give
\(S_j\le c^j-1\) and \(S_j\ge ac^{j+1}/2\) for \(j\ge1\), using
\(12/5<c<21/8\), \(5/14<a<1/2\). The latter induction uses
\(1+c-c^2/2>0\), which is positive throughout that rational interval.

For the actual polynomial

\[
p_m(\lambda)=\prod_{i=0}^{m-1}
[1-\lambda(1+ac^i)]^{2^{m-i-1}},
\]

the three frequency cases give the claimed uniform bound

\[
D_m\sup_{0\le\lambda\le1}\lambda p_m(\lambda)^2
\le9/16.
\tag{21}
\]

The all-positive case is at most \(3/(2e)<9/16\). The all-negative case is at most \(2(5/12)^4<9/16\). If the first negative level is \(k\in\{1,\ldots,m-1\}\), set \(r=m-k\), \(z=ac^k\lambda\), \(T=czp_k(\lambda)\). The exact multiplicities give

\[
|p_m(\lambda)|\le T^{2^r}/(zc^{r+1}),\quad
T\le2/e<3/4,\quad z>a/(1+a).
\]

Hence the normalized value is less than
\[
\frac{2(1+a)}{\beta^2}c^{-r}T^{2^{r+1}}
<\frac9{20}<\frac9{16}.
\]

Polynomial roots and \(\lambda=0\) give zero. This confirms the draft's algebra analytically; it is not a formal verification or a full-class upper bound.

The parent reports the accepted comparison
\(B_{2^m-1}/D_m<47/50\) for the same inherited-parameter word and \(m\ge1536\), citing project fact 931b9653ce0f7201. That fact was not independently re-read through the fact service in this invocation. Conditional on that comparison, (21) gives

\[
B_{2^m-1}\sup_\lambda\lambda p_m(\lambda)^2<423/800,
\qquad B_{2^m-1}/D_m<47/50.
\tag{22}
\]

Thus this really is a word where the stated Huber and complete scalar-quadratic pool fall short of the target, under the recorded benchmark comparison. On this word, (15) or (20) says that any successful replenished witness must already contain a centered tangent history with the required original-scale error. The common component itself cannot supply the missing improvement.

This does not bound all centered tangent histories on that word. No such full-family upper bound was proved, and no negative conclusion about \(W_n\) or the original conjecture follows.

## 5. Original-target closure status

The proposed translation exists exactly under its stated common-normal condition, preserves every old nonzero row and every physical step, and has the complete value and radius costs (5). For the actual orthogonal recursion the closest-minimum check is complete. Its whole output is dominated by a real centered tangent trajectory or Huber, not by an unproved scalar state oracle.

The attempted negative-correlation repair (17) is also a real static construction. Its full minimum cone does not rescue the radius: 0 is closest, and the exact pointwise domination (20) applies. Repeating these common shifts or common lifts cannot establish a sharp bound unless the required centered-history estimate has already been obtained.

That remaining all-word estimate was not proved in this invocation. No schedule-independent \(1-o(1)\) comparison with the exact \(B_n\) recurrence, and no rigorous full-class asymptotic separation, was obtained. The original target remains OPEN. These are limitations of the investigated mechanisms, not a claim that the conjecture is false.

For any prescribed \(L>0\), multiplying the displayed functions by \(L\) restores \(L\)-smoothness and leaves their normalized GD trajectories and \(W=2R\) ratios unchanged.

The one-call/two-line allocation ends here. No truth store, budget, Goal, service, worker, publication, finalization or formal-verification state was changed.

# R6: a real recursive adversary with replenished retained height

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926. Original Goal ACTIVE/OPEN. Fresh ONE native analytical invocation, gpt-6-astra/ultra, consult OFF, at most TWO substantive proof lines, mathematical programs/arrays/solvers/formal submissions ZERO. Parent owns budget. Read OPERATOR.md, original PROBLEM.md and R5_DIRECT_HANDOFF_20261008.md. No prior grant is reopened.

The latest human priority is to close the original question using independent mathematical judgment and inspiration from Zhang--Jiang, rather than accumulate equivalent formulations or fixed-horizon examples. The exact affirmative target is: for every epsilon>0, all sufficiently large n and ALL predetermined h>=0, W_n(h)>=(1-epsilon)/B_n, threshold independent of h. W=2R; original last iterate and original selected-minimizer radius. C0=B0=1, C_n=max Omega(C_k,C_l), B_n=max G(C_k,B_l), every integer split k+l=n-1. Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt(x*x+6*x*y+y*y))/2; G(x,y)=2*x+y/2+sqrt(y*y+8*x*y)/2. A full-class asymptotic separation is also a valid resolution. Restricted-family uppers are not one.

## Concrete starting point

Read the COMPLETE joint_history_closure_20261008_r5/PROOF_ATTEMPT.md and HANDOFF.md. Its selected-origin lower constructions are real static smooth functions. For child history (v_j,b_j), M=max<v0,b_j>, H=min<v_j-b_j,b_j>, eta=H/M, a=||v0||^2/M, z=2F_end/M. Prepending mass C-1 and a bridge 1+d through the orthogonal hull gives a'=C+v+a/J, z'=z/J, eta'=eta/J, J=1+C/v, legal when eta>=1-d/v. The actual local G root is matched by a real Huber child. That equality also occurs at the global Huber benchmark; it is not yet evidence of recursive completeness. Repeated height depletion is the material issue.

## Proposed genuinely new closure mechanism, not a premise

For a fixed finite hull K=conv(0,g_i), if an actual vector e satisfies <e,g_i>=1 for every nonzero retained vertex, translating the ENTIRE trajectory x_i to x_i+t e preserves all nonzero-vertex projection normal differences. It changes the rows against 0, terminal value, and original radius. This observation is a lead to derive, not a granted theorem. It might replenish the height without paying a new auxiliary clock at every node. The original radius becomes ||x0+t e||^2 and must be counted exactly; do not silently use the old radius or assume 0 is the closest minimizer. The whole polar minimizer cone matters if using a closest-point improvement. All translations and body choices are made before the run, never switched at a checkpoint.

Use this or a better actual geometric mechanism to prove a recursively stable family of child histories with joint boundary data, then obtain the original all-word bound. The point is to make the missing choice/height theorem TRUE, not simply define another reachability oracle. A shared normal may not exist after every proposed composition; prove its existence and norm, all cross rows, attained minimum, and terminal value. Optimize or telescope the radius/height costs across the entire actual tree. An abstract PSD Gram with no valid old-query realization is insufficient.

Global Huber gives 1/(1+2sum h); scalar quadratics give sup_{lambda in[0,1]}lambda product(1-h_i lambda)^2. These are fallback ACTUAL witnesses, not a presumed complete pool. For words they fail to cover, select the actual cuts and histories from the whole known word. Every prefix/suffix still consists of its actual physical steps. Replacing actual mass by C_k or B_l without a proof is forbidden. Obtain uniform losses compared with the exact recurrence, not merely the silver exponent or a dyadic-phase constant.

If the first geometric choice has an intrinsic obstruction, use at most one genuinely different repair that addresses it (e.g. coupled negative correlations while keeping a common normal), and attempt the same full closure. A family loss on an already baseline-covered word does not reject the combined portfolio. If full closure fails, give the decisive mathematical reason and exact remaining inequality, not a new equivalent target presented as progress. Do not spend this call on another fixed-step toy model or a generic representation theorem.

## Bounded outputs

Only primary-source/text reads, manual analytical reasoning, Markdown writes and hashes. No mathematical programs, numerical data reanalysis, calculators, new agents/models, paid consult, service/worker actions, private memory, truth-store edits, publication, finalization, revocation, shutdown or Goal mutation. Write self-contained PROOF_ATTEMPT.md and HANDOFF.md in this new directory, label all new mathematics UNVERIFIED. Identify whether a full original-target proof was obtained. One invocation, at most two lines; then STOP for parent full-read and closeout. No automatic renewal.

# R9 retained-reserve handoff

**UNVERIFIED. Original target OPEN. No all-depth U certificate.**

Allocation `b942008eeb852c04`: one analytical invocation, two substantive lines, mathematical programs/arrays/solvers/formal submissions zero. Parent owns and closes the ledger. Full derivations and scope checks are in PROOF_ATTEMPT.md in this folder.

1. The parent-supplied discounted-mass identity is correct. For M=d+2kappa S and the actual canonical mixture z, its exact additional term is +2(1-kappa)S||P(y/d)||^2. Accepted primitive H_1/H_2 certificates give eta=1 retained bases for every d>=1 and every kappa in [1/2,1]. Accepted H_3 only gives nonnegativity of the discounted gap by monotonicity; its discounted retained fraction is not proved.

2. The full actual parent has s=S_j, b=1+k, h=s+b, A=1+2kappa s, alpha=A-s, D=d+2kappa s, M=D+2kappa h. Its feasible mixture range is theta<=min(1,b/alpha). The exact identity is report (2.10)-(2.11). Using the left gap creates the negative square -2(1-kappa)As||p||^2 at the same time as the positive prefix square +2(1-kappa)Mh||r||^2. At kappa=16/17 the actual root coefficient remains positive, with Theta/(hC)>991/5196 for j>=2, but b/alpha<1 still rules out theta=1 as a guaranteed convex mixture. Formula (2.14) gives a conditional quadratic payment; no favorable eta or all-depth closure is asserted.

3. The prefix remainder R_theta is not the complete parent's canonical remainder R_all. With u_R=r-z_R, n_R=w-D r, and sigma=2kappa-1, their exact transport uses

       t=M(z_all-z_theta)=-D u_R+sigma h(q-r)-theta A u,
       n_theta=n_R-sigma h r-theta A u,
       M(R_all-R_theta)=-2<n_theta,t>+||t||^2.

   The attempted parent retained-fraction identity is (3.4). Its correction includes the negative actual normal reserve 2sigma h<n_R,q-r>. This is not paid merely by labeling RR nonnegative or retaining a fraction of RR.

4. An actual whole-parent K=[0,1] trajectory proves that ||q-r||^2 cannot be controlled by RR alone. Start at x=s+b+(1+a). The left child has gradient one throughout, the bridge puts the right start at 1+a, and the first right step hits zero; all remaining steps stay zero. No quantified joint vector/normal reserve was propagated. No kappa_m>=16/17 theorem or full W bound has been obtained. <!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002836 -->
# R9: discounted child reserves and the complete parent reserve

**UNVERIFIED research draft. The all-depth certificate is not closed.**

Allocation `b942008eeb852c04`: one native analytical invocation, two substantive proof lines, mathematical programs/arrays/solvers/formal submissions zero. The parent owns the ledger. The calculations below are manual exact derivations. None has been submitted to the Danus verifier.

The supplied discounted-mass identity is correct. Its use in a parent introduces a negative left-endpoint square at the same time as the positive prefix square. The full parent maximization remainder also contains a right endpoint-to-initial-auxiliary difference and an associated projection normal. A bound retaining only a fraction of the canonical child maximization remainder has not been propagated. No coefficient sequence with final kappa at least 16/17 has been proved.

## 1. Target and accepted premises

For a predetermined nonnegative word h of length n, retain

    x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L) grad f(x_i),
    W_n(h)=sup 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2),
    W_n^*=inf_(h>=0) W_n(h),  W=2R.

The supremum is over every finite Euclidean dimension, every convex differentiable L-smooth function with attained minimum, every selected minimizer, and every distinct start. The target is whether W_n^*/U_n^ZJ tends to one, where U_n^ZJ=1/B_n is the original benchmark with every integer split retained.

Fix one enclosing integer m>=1536 and, throughout all children, set

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j),
    S_j=sum H_j, C_j=1+S_j, D_m=1+2S_m.

Each H_j has length 2^j-1. Accepted `931b9653ce0f7201` gives B_(2^m-1)/D_m<47/50. Therefore a proved full-class bound W(H_m)<=17/(16D_m) for all sufficiently large m would give a strict 799/800 subsequence separation. That full-class bound remains unproved.

Accepted `1e0bf3f41457d8e4` supplies finite-word equality of the full original supremum with the supremum over origin-vertex projection-simplex envelopes. It also supplies the actual same-m H_3 U law for every compact convex body containing zero, every start, and every d>=1. Part B proves primitive positivity for H_1 and H_2 and gives exact identities; it does not assume primitive positivity for H_3 or higher levels. Its Part C retains the original selected-minimum radius.

Throughout the new derivations fix one nonempty compact convex K containing zero, in a finite-dimensional real Euclidean space, and write

    P=P_K,
    F(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2),
    U_t(x)=max_(g in K)(2<x,g>-t||g||^2), t>0.

Every physical and auxiliary projection uses this same K. The gradient is P and the full minimum set is K^polar. For any selected x_star in K^polar,

    U_t(x)<=||x-x_star||^2/t.

Thus an all-body final U law at d=1 would transfer, by the accepted supremum equality, to the full W class. A result for one body or one trajectory cannot make that transfer.

## 2. First proof line: derive the discounted identity and its actual parent payment

### 2.1 The discounted identity, including all signs

Let a complete actual finite nonnegative word have mass S, input x and endpoint y. For d>=1 and 1/2<=kappa<=1 put

    delta=1-kappa, M=d+2kappa S,
    q=P(y/d), p0=P(x/M),
    z=[x-y+(M-S)q]/M.

The physical displacement is x-y=sum_i h_i g_i. Since M-S=d+(2kappa-1)S>=0, z is a convex mixture in K. Define

    phi_t(v,g)=2<v,g>-t||g||^2,
    Rem(v,t,g)=U_t(v)-phi_t(v,g).

For g in K and p=P(v/t), expansion at the true maximizer gives

    Rem(v,t,g)=t||p-g||^2+2<v-tp,p-g> >=0.           (2.1)

The normal term is nonnegative because <v-tp,g-p><=0. In particular no arbitrary nonnegative value is assigned to a remainder.

Here x-Mz=y-(M-S)q, whence

    M phi_M(x,z)=||x||^2-||y-(M-S)q||^2.

Subtracting M U_d(y)=M(2<y,q>-d||q||^2) yields

    M[phi_M(x,z)-U_d(y)]
      =||x||^2-||y+S q||^2+M(d-M+2S)||q||^2.

Since d-M+2S=2delta S,

    U_M(x)-U_d(y)
      =[||x||^2-||y+S q||^2]/M
        +2delta S||q||^2+Rem(x,M,z).                (2.2)

Let g=P(y), V=||x||^2-||y+Sg||^2, and

    Lrad=2S<y,g-q>+S^2(||g||^2-||q||^2).

The two maximizer comparisons at trailing parameters 1 and d give

    ||g||^2-||q||^2 <= 2<y,g-q>
       <= d(||g||^2-||q||^2).

For d>1 these inequalities imply both differences are nonnegative; at d=1 they vanish. Thus Lrad>=0, and the parent-supplied identity is exactly

    U_M(x)-U_d(y)
      =(V+Lrad)/M+2delta S||q||^2+Rem(x,M,z).       (2.3)

At kappa=1 it reduces to the accepted identity. For S=0, z=q=p0 and every term vanishes.

The accepted primitive H_1 and H_2 certificates, together with (2.3), give the genuine retained base

    U_(d+2kappa S_j)(x)-U_d(T_Hj x)
       >= Rem(x,d+2kappa S_j,z),  j=1,2,            (2.4)

for every allowed kappa, every d>=1, every K and every start. Thus eta=1 is justified at these two bases. For H_3 the accepted unrelaxed U law also implies the discounted gap is nonnegative, because U_t decreases as t increases. That monotonicity does not establish (2.4) for the new canonical remainder at H_3.

### 2.2 The true discounted parent

Take the actual parent H_(j+1). Write s=S_j, C=1+s, k=a c^j, b=1+k, h=s+b=C+k, and S=2s+b. Its left child runs from x to v, its physical bridge uses p=P(v), and its right child runs from w=v-bp to y. Both children use the exact H_j and its enclosing parameters.

For one fixed kappa in [1/2,1] and d>=1 define

    delta=1-kappa, sigma=2kappa-1,
    A=1+2kappa s, alpha=A-s=1+sigma s,
    D=d+2kappa s, M=D+2kappa h=d+2kappa S,
    r=P(w/D), q=P(y/d), ell=P(x/A),
    z_L=[x-v+alpha p]/A,
    z_R=[w-y+(D-s)q]/D,
    z_P=[x-w+(M-h)r]/M.

All three comparison points are in K. Their physical-gradient weights and the displayed auxiliary weights are nonnegative and sum to their denominators. Put

    u=ell-z_L, n_L=x-A ell,
    RL=Rem(x,A,z_L)=A||u||^2+2<n_L,u>,
    RR=Rem(w,D,z_R), RP=Rem(x,M,z_P),
    E_L=U_A(x)-U_1(v), E_R=U_D(w)-U_d(y),
    Psi=U_M(x)-U_d(y),
    V_L=||x||^2-||v+s p||^2.

The left identity (2.3), at trailing one, is

    V_L=A E_L-A RL-2delta A s||p||^2.               (2.5)

This is the negative left square that must accompany any use of the positive discounted prefix square.

The two actual bridge normals are

    Np=<v-p,p-r> >=0,
    Nr=<w-D r,r-p> >=0.

Set

    Theta=h[2CD/(D+k)-h]
         =h[CD-k(C+D+k)]/(D+k),
    J=Theta||p-r||^2
      +2h(1+C/(D+k))Np+2hC/(D+k)Nr.               (2.6)

These coefficients use the new D, not the old undiscounted value. To check the identity without importing the old M, put e=p-r and X=v+s p. The actual bridge gives w+h r=X-h e, and

    Np+Nr=(D+k)<p,e>-D||e||^2,
    <X,e>=C D||e||^2/(D+k)
           +(1+C/(D+k))Np+C Nr/(D+k).

Consequently ||x||^2-||w+h r||^2=V_L+J. Applying (2.2) to the actual prefix of mass h, with trailing D, gives

    M Psi=V_L+J+2delta M h||r||^2+M RP+M E_R.       (2.7)

Together (2.5) and (2.7) give both signed discount terms exactly. Neither child endpoint has been reinitialized.

### 2.3 Feasible auxiliary mixture and the relative-reserve debt

For a scalar theta consider

    z_theta=z_P+(theta A/M)u.

Writing G=x-v=sum_i h_i g_i for the complete actual left displacement gives

    M z_theta=(1-theta)G+(b-theta alpha)p
               +(M-h)r+theta A ell.                (2.8)

Since M-h=D+sigma h>0, the explicit sufficient feasibility range is

    0<=theta<=min(1,b/alpha).                        (2.9)

Let R_theta=Rem(x,M,z_theta). A direct square expansion gives

    M[phi_M(x,z_theta)-phi_M(x,z_P)]
       =2theta A<w-(M-h)r,u>-theta^2 A^2||u||^2.

Also x-Az_L=v-alpha p, so

    RL=2<v-alpha p,u>-A||u||^2.

Combining these equalities with (2.5)-(2.7) yields

    M Psi=A E_L+M E_R+J+M R_theta+T_theta,           (2.10)

where

    T_theta=-(1-theta)A RL
       +theta(1-theta)A^2||u||^2
       +2theta A<(alpha-b)p-(M-h)r,u>
       +2delta[Mh||r||^2-As||p||^2].                (2.11)

Equivalently, the first two terms are

    -(1-theta)^2 A^2||u||^2
       -2(1-theta)A<n_L,u>.

The left normal is nonnegative and has a negative coefficient unless theta=1. The mixed vector is exactly

    (alpha-b)(p-r)-(M-A)r.                          (2.12)

Thus the positive discounted r square cannot simply be appended to R8's payment. The coefficient of the p square, the left mixture capacity, the trailing root parameter, and the mixed r component all change.

For kappa=16/17, the root coefficient itself remains positive at every j>=2. Here are elementary uniform bounds. The exact mass solution is

    C_j=v0 c^j+(1-v0)2^j, v0=a/(c-2), 0<v0<1,

which follows by substitution in C_(j+1)=2C_j+a c^j and C_0=1. Also v0=beta/[c(c-2)] is between zero and one because c>lambda and c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta. Hence k/C<=c-2. The inequality sqrt(2)<99/70 and epsilon<=1/6144 give

    c<(169/70)(6145/6144)<29/12,

where the last comparison is 12462060<12472320. Hence k/C<5/12. Since a>1/3 and beta>9/10, C_2=4+2a+beta>5, and C>5 for j>=2. Therefore

    D/C >= [1+(32/17)(C-1)]/C >29/17,
    Theta/(hC)>7/12-170/433=991/5196>0.             (2.13)

The two normal coefficients in J are positive as well. However,

    alpha-b=(15C+2)/17-1-k
             >(95C-180)/204>0.

Thus even with the allowed discount, the guaranteed mixture range has theta<=b/alpha<1. The shortcut theta=1 still has a negative bridge-gradient coefficient in (2.8). No claim that it lies in K is made.

One can state a precise sufficient payment for this first attempt. Suppose, as an additional proved child hypothesis, E_L>=eta RL with 0<=eta<=1, and E_R>=0. If theta obeys (2.9) and theta>=1-eta, the nonnegative part of RL can be used to obtain

    M Psi >= M R_theta
       +2h(1+C/(D+k))Np+2hC/(D+k)Nr+Q_eta,

where, with e=p-r,

    Q_eta=A^2[eta-(1-theta)^2]||u||^2
       +(Theta-2delta As)||e||^2
       +2delta(Mh-As)||r||^2-4delta As<e,r>
       +2theta A(alpha-b)<e,u>
       -2theta A(M-A)<r,u>.                         (2.14)

The inequality follows from RL>=A||u||^2 and A(eta-1+theta)>=0. No coefficients have been asserted positive without that hypothesis. A positive semidefinite scalar coefficient matrix for (2.14) would be a sufficient one-parent U payment. Even such a payment would not retain a fraction of the *complete parent's* canonical remainder, which is required for another level. That is the second line below.

I did not prove an all-body eta for H_3 and beyond that both pays (2.11) and propagates. Taking eta=1 from the H_2 base and silently repeating it would assume the missing theorem. Taking eta=0 from the accepted U law leaves the relative-reserve debt unpaid. Equations (2.10)-(2.14) are exact or explicitly conditional statements, not a closed recursion.

## 3. Second proof line: transport the complete parent remainder, retaining the new vector and normal

The first line could at best pay Psi. A retained-reserve induction needs to compare its parent reserve to the canonical reserve in the proposed induction statement. These are different actual comparison points.

Define

    z_all=[x-y+(M-S)q]/M,
    R_all=Rem(x,M,z_all),
    u_R=r-z_R,
    n_R=w-D r,
    v_R=q-r.

The notation v_R in this section is a vector, not a child endpoint or a primitive energy. Every quantity is from the already fixed physical parent and its actual auxiliary projections.

Using the complete physical right displacement D z_R=w-y+(D-s)q, coefficient collection gives

    t:=M(z_all-z_theta)
      =-D u_R+sigma h v_R-theta A u.                (3.1)

Indeed the coefficient of q after eliminating the right displacement is M-D-h=sigma h. The coefficient of r is then the opposite one. The same calculation at the prefix point gives

    n_theta:=x-M z_theta
       =n_R-sigma h r-theta A u.                   (3.2)

Neither z_theta nor its residual n_theta is presumed to be an optimizer/normal pair. Both z_theta and z_all are in K, so their maximization remainders exist and are nonnegative, but that does not sign their difference.

Expanding phi_M at z_theta gives the exact remainder transport

    M(R_all-R_theta)=-2<n_theta,t>+||t||^2.          (3.3)

For any proposed parent fraction eta' in [0,1], (2.10) becomes

    M(Psi-eta' R_all)
       =A E_L+M E_R+J+(1-eta')M R_theta+T_theta
          +eta'[2<n_theta,t>-||t||^2].              (3.4)

This is the complete same-body composition identity for the attempted fraction, not a projection in a difference space. It retains the actual parent canonical remainder rather than replacing it by the prefix remainder R_theta.

For clarity about the new normal term put t0=-D u_R+sigma h v_R, so t=t0-theta A u. Then the correction in (3.4) is

    2<n_theta,t>-||t||^2
      =2<n_R,t0>-2theta A<n_R,u>
         -2sigma h<r,t0>+2sigma h theta A<r,u>
         +theta^2 A^2||u||^2-||t0||^2.              (3.5)

In particular

    2<n_R,t0>=-2D<n_R,u_R>+2sigma h<n_R,q-r>.

The last inner product is nonpositive by the actual normal at r, so it is a *negative* multiple of the nonnegative reserve

    N_(r,q)=<n_R,r-q> >=0.

It cannot be discarded as favorable. The first term also uses the real right-child maximization normal, which is part of RR=D||u_R||^2+2<n_R,u_R>. The full E_R remains available in (3.4); retaining only E_R>=eta RR does not by itself sign the other terms in (3.5).

### 3.1 An actual parent trajectory with RR=0 and q-r nonzero

Here is an exact admissible test showing that the additional vector in (3.1) cannot be bounded by RR alone. It does not refute the U law or a retained-fraction inequality.

Take K=[0,1] on the real line. Its envelope is

    F(z)=0                    for z<=0,
         z^2/2                for 0<=z<=1,
         z-1/2                for z>=1.

It is globally convex, differentiable and 1-smooth, with gradient P(z)=max(0,min(z,1)) and minimum cone (-infinity,0]. Fix any j>=1 and let ell0=1+a be the first step of its actual H_j. Keep the actual parent bridge b and child mass s. Choose the original parent start

    x=s+b+ell0.

Every left-child iterate is at least v=b+ell0>1, so every left gradient is one and the complete left child ends at v. The physical bridge sends v to w=ell0. The first step of the right child uses gradient one and sends w exactly to zero. Every subsequent original right-child step stays at zero, because its gradient is zero. Thus y=0 and q=P(y/d)=0 for every d>=1.

Since s>=ell0 and kappa>=1/2,

    D=d+2kappa s>ell0,
    r=P(w/D)=ell0/D in (0,1),
    z_R=[w-y+(D-s)q]/D=ell0/D=r.

Therefore

    RR=0, u_R=0, n_R=0, q-r=-ell0/D !=0,
    E_R=U_D(w)-U_d(0)=ell0^2/D=D||q-r||^2>0.       (3.6)

All points are from one actual parent run; there is no independent resetting of the right child's start. This example contradicts any proposed uniform estimate ||q-r||^2<=c RR with a finite c, even at a fixed child level and fixed trailing parameter. It also shows why retaining the entire right gap matters: that gap is positive precisely when its canonical remainder vanishes in this example. It does not contradict an estimate involving E_R, and no impossibility claim for such an estimate is made.

### 3.2 What is still required

Equation (3.4) needs a quantitative payment of the actual vector v_R and the actual normal N_(r,q), together with the mixed u terms. One possible stronger child statement would retain these quantities jointly with RR. Neither its base constants nor an all-body composition were established here. They cannot be introduced as free nonnegative reserves with convenient independent values.

The identity (3.4) is also not proof that a retained-fraction statement is false. Its signed terms are correlated by all original projection rows. A negative direction of a coefficient matrix with independent u, u_R, r and q would not be a realizable function counterexample. The only claimed counterexample in this line is the explicitly scoped bound-by-RR-alone failure in (3.6).

No extrapolated point was silently projected back into K. Every comparison point used above has its displayed convex-mixture membership proof. No difference gradients were treated as a new smooth-convex gradient history, and no all-level primitive assumption or R7 terminal potential was used.

## 4. Final extraction and why it is not available

If an all-body induction actually established, at j=m and d=1,

    U_1(T_Hm x)<=U_(1+2kappa_m S_m)(x),
    kappa_m>=16/17,

then for every selected x_star in K^polar,

    2F(T_Hm x)<=||x-x_star||^2/[1+(32/17)S_m]
               <=17||x-x_star||^2/[16(1+2S_m)].

The last comparison is 16+32S_m<=17+32S_m after multiplying positive denominators. Accepted finite-word supremum universality would give the required full W bound, and accepted `931b9653ce0f7201` would yield the 799/800 subsequence gap. There is no change of original radius, last iterate, normalization, or hierarchy in this implication.

The missing premise remains the all-body all-depth induction, including a proved retained fraction or another sufficient vector reserve. This report establishes no such premise, no full-class upper bound at height m, and no original-target resolution.

# R9: quantitatively retain the actual child reserve and close the full-class bound

Fresh ONE gpt-6-astra/ultra analytical invocation, <=TWO substantive proof lines, mathematical programs/arrays/solvers/formal submissions ZERO. Consult OFF, parent owns ledger. Read OPERATOR.md, COMPLETE PROBLEM.md, R8_DIRECT_HANDOFF_20261009.md, and COMPLETE matched_child_u_closure_20261009_r8/PROOF_ATTEMPT.md. Human prioritizes direct original-target closure inspired by Zhang--Jiang, not another equivalent recurrence or fixed-horizon toy. All old grants closed.

## Exact original-target consequence

For enclosing m>=1536, keep eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt2)(1+eps), a=beta/c in H0 empty, H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), every child using the SAME enclosing parameters. S_j=sum H_j, D_m=1+2S_m. Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 gives B_(2^m-1)/D_m<47/50. A full-function-class W(H_m)<=17/(16D_m) for every sufficiently large m would refute the original limit-one problem by a strict799/800 subsequence gap. W=2R and original radii/last iterate are mandatory. That premise is still unproved.

Read accepted1e0bf3f41457d8e4 statement and complete PartB/C. It gives finite-word full-class projection-simplex supremum universality and actual H3 all-body U law. It does NOT give all-depth primitive positivity. For one fixed compact convex K containing0, use

    P=P_K, F_K(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2),
    U_d(x)=max_(g in K)(2<x,g>-d||g||^2).

All physical and auxiliary points use this same K. The full min set is K-polar and U_d(x)<=dist(x,K-polar)^2/d. Hence a true all-body U estimate at d=1 transfers to the full W class through the accepted supremum theorem. No restricted-body upper is a full W bound.

## What R8 left unpaid

The complete R8 report gives the exact parent identity with child U gaps E_L,E_R, root square J, actual parent remainder and a signed T_theta. Its feasible mixture only guarantees0<=theta<=b/C<1. The troublesome term is a NEGATIVE multiple of the actual left maximization remainder RL, plus a mixed r component not paid by ||p-r||^2 alone. Settingtheta1 generates a negative bridge coefficient and is not a valid convex-combination argument. Difference gradients are also not automatically a convex-gradient history; do not apply a child theorem in that fictitious space.

The proposed genuinely new line is to prove how much of an ACTUAL child remainder its U gap retains, and to use the genuine slack permitted by17/16. This must be an independently true all-body inequality, with a proved composition and uniform constants. Simply assuming a favorable retained fraction restates the gap.

## Explicit discounted-mass identity to check and use

For a complete physical child h of massS, actual x->y, trailing d>=1, and a chosen kappa in[1/2,1], put

    M=d+2*kappa*S, q=P(y/d), p=P(x/M),
    z=[x-y+(M-S)q]/M.

The physical displacement x-y=sum h_i*g_i and M-S>=0 make z a genuine convex mixture in K. Define

    R_kappa=Rem(x,M,z)=M||p-z||^2+2<x-Mp,p-z> >=0,
    V=||x||^2-||y+S*P(y)||^2,
    Lrad=2S<y,P(y)-q>+S^2(||P(y)||^2-||q||^2)>=0.

Parent manual expansion, UNVERIFIED until rederived:

    U_M(x)-U_d(y)
     =(V+Lrad)/M +2*(1-kappa)*S*||q||^2 +R_kappa.       (I)

The extra positive q-square is the actual price allowed by reducing the effective accumulated mass; it was absent from the unrelaxed R8 payment. Taking kappa=16/17, a proved nonnegative gap at j=m,d=1 would give W<=1/[1+(32/17)S_m] <=17/(16D_m). Thus no new benchmark or approximate word is involved.

One concrete route is an inequality of the form

    U_(d+2*kappa_j*S_j)(x)-U_d(T_Hj x)
       >= eta_j(d)*R_(kappa_j),

with explicit nonnegative retained fraction eta_j(d) and kappa_m>=16/17, ACTUALLY proved at the base and propagated using the full same-body normals and actual parent coefficients. The kappa_j and eta_j are NOT granted variables with presumed feasible values. Prove their existence/formulas and the full composition. You may instead derive a different actual vector-valued reserve that closes the same final full-class bound. An endpoint inequality with an unsupported scalar loss profile is not a repair.

At kappa=1, (I) reduces to the accepted exact decomposition. A condition E_L>=eta*RL would control the specific debt in R8, but its validity is a new mathematical statement, not a consequence of E_L>=0. At kappa<1 the p/r square balances and convex-mixture weights must all be recalculated, not carried over by analogy. If an extrapolated point is projected back into K, prove membership and ALL projection corrections rather than discarding them.

## Required closure and known exclusions

Try to prove the exact all-depth inequality for every compact K, every initial point and all trailing parameters actually required by the induction. Retain the same original physical steps, including all child endpoints, and no arbitrary reinitialization. A successful statement must yield the final uniform asymptotic full W certificate; no finite table, presumed rank, or hypothesized positive quadratic remainder completes it.

The R7 q_j=(1+eps)^(-j) all-level endpoint potential is FALSE on a real asymmetric-Huber H7 trajectory for every enclosing m>=1536. Its final P_m was not refuted. Read its proof if using a related terminal strengthening. The current line is not a request merely to add a startup constant to q_j. If a candidate retained-fraction inequality fails, exhibit an ACTUAL admissible trajectory or keep the failure scoped to the chosen certificate. At most one genuinely changed second line is available; do not exhaust it on several equivalent unsupported profiles.

The newly inspected primary Peppy paper https://arxiv.org/html/2609.35762 Sections2 and AppendicesC/D may suggest grouping full certificates into a small basis of actual vectors, but no low-rank completeness is assumed. No Peppy code, workflow/model, symbolic program or old numerical array is authorized in this call. Its role is methodological only.

## Outputs and resource cap

One invocation/<=two substantive lines/program-array-solver-formal0. Text/primary-source reads, manual reasoning, Markdown/hashes only.
