# A growing shifted-circle chain: actual coupled dynamics and the unpaid input flux

Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED ANALYTICAL DRAFT. The growing policy passes the
scale screen. Its complete retained terminal payoff is NOT bounded sharply
enough here to prove either a benchmark witness or a complete obstruction.
A specific nonnegative input flux remains unpaid. No formal gate was
allocated; no assertion below has been promoted to a fact.

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; worker max3; one round after baseline59,
completed ceiling60 / attempted ceiling61. TASK.md and guidance
7b221a98307db859 govern. Frozen-policy finding: eec80a72df26e757.
No construction repair was used. Previous drafts4c4a21a484564bdc and
ced9f5595b446c1d are context, not accepted premises. All ingredients below
are derived jointly. The only accepted mathematical input is the original
benchmark/ray conclusion of fact04219554e41d9750.

## 1. Frozen policy and the actual problem left open

For each integer m>=16 put

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), tau=1/4, k=floor(sqrt(m)), J=m-k,
    beta=1/k, eta_word=1-1/(4m), c=lambda*(1+1/(4m)),
    a_word=eta_word/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), b_j=1+a_word*c^j,
    S_j=sum H_j, n_m=2^m-1.

All children retain the SAME enclosing m and eta_word,c,a_word. Circular
beta differs from eta_word. Select precisely the first occurrences of
levels J,J+1,...,m-1: k physical steps at zero-based positions
2^(J+i-1)-1 for i=1,...,k. No other step is changed or removed. The
unchanged word is exactly

    (H_J,b_J,H_J,b_(J+1),H_(J+1),b_(J+2),...,
                                      H_(m-2),b_(m-1),H_(m-1)).       (1.1)

Thus ordinary block masses are s_1=S_J, s_i=S_(J+i-2) for 2<=i<=k,
and s_(k+1)=S_(m-1). Selected step i is b_(J+i-1). These steps exceed2
for all sufficiently large m, the range used for all eventual conclusions.

The original benchmark, never recomputed here, is

    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=r<n) Omega(C_r,C_(n-r-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=r<n) G(C_r,B_(n-r-1)),
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2, U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

Every integer split r is included. Fact04219554e41d9750 gives

    0<Z_1=lim_m B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m<infinity.                       (1.2)

For the static convex1-smooth F below with minimum0, write
E_m=2F(x_(n_m))/dist(x_0,argmin F)^2. Multiplying F by any advertised
L>0 gives x^+=x-(h/L)grad(LF)=x-h grad F and the same payoff. This is
a particular lower witness for W_n(h), not an identity for its supremum.
The construction gives the EXACT decomposition

    B_(n_m) E_m = T_m+B_(n_m) V_m/R_m^2,                         (1.3)
    T_m -> T_infty:=Z_1*exp(-17/4)/(2lambda)>0.                   (1.4)

V_m retains ALL k circular values at the actual terminal iterate; R_m
is the true closest initial distance. Equation(1.4) is a scale screen,
not the requested retained-history result. The unpaid term is identified
in Sections7--10, including all ordinary steps and large overshoots.

## 2. Preliminary scale screen

Sections3--6 jointly prove, uniformly over these k blocks,

    C_beta=beta+sqrt(1+2beta),
    M_i=C_beta*s_i/2+O(k^2 exp(2k)), R_m^2->1,
    D_(k+1)->(lambda-2)exp(-2), sigma_1~2/(C_beta*S_J).           (2.1)

The overlap smoothness bound remains at most1 for every k, so dimension
does not dilute the normalization. The final Huber term consequently
has the positive benchmark-scale limit(1.4), with no loss exponential
in k. These statements are proved below, not assumed as premises.

The retained caps and radius alone also do not force a deficit. For the
SAME function, the comparison point z=(0,-1,0,...,0) has norm1 and

    Phi_1(z_1,z_2)=sigma_1-(1+beta^2)*sigma_1^2/2,

while every later circular component is zero. Therefore

    B_(n_m)*Phi_1(z_1,z_2)/R_m^2
                  ~2Z_1*exp(-1/4)*lambda^k ->infinity.          (2.2)

This is solely a cap/radius screen. The point z is NOT asserted to be
on the actual trajectory. It shows why a geometric cap bound cannot
reject this policy as trivially diluted. The actual terminal V_m still
has to be paid; neither(2.2) nor(1.4) is a retained-history solution.

## 3. Circular envelope and every local physical step

For 0<beta<=1/4 set

    p(q)=beta+sqrt((1+beta)^2-(q+beta)^2), 0<=q<=1,
    v(q)=(p(q),-q), K=conv({0} union {v(q):0<=q<=1}).

The disk of center(beta,beta), radius1+beta contains0 because
2beta^2<=(1+beta)^2. Its radial vector(p(q)-beta,-q-beta) supports
K at v(q). The endpoints are(C_beta,0),(beta,-1), and the horizontal
segment from0 to(C_beta,0) is in K. Hence

    0<=p<=C_beta, 0<=q<=1, p>=beta q,
    (p,0) in K whenever(p,-q) in K.                              (3.1)

For compact convex C containing0 define
E_C(r)=max_(g in C){<g,r>-||g||^2/2}. Completing the square gives
the unique maximizer Pi_C(r), the Euclidean projection. Its inequalities
at r,s give ||Pi_C(r)-Pi_C(s)||^2<=<Pi_C(r)-Pi_C(s),r-s>.
Comparing maximization formulas at r,r+d bounds the increment after
subtracting <Pi_C(r),d> by ||d||^2. Thus E_C is differentiable, convex,
nonnegative and1-smooth with gradient Pi_C. Its zero set is the polar
cone: a positive support value gives a positive objective at a small
positive multiple of the supporting vector. The cone of K is generated
by its endpoints, so

    E_(sigma K)(U,V)=0 iff U<=0 and beta U-V<=0.                  (3.2)

Define Hub_delta(t) to be0 for t<=0, t^2/2 for 0<=t<=delta, and
delta*t-delta^2/2 for t>=delta; its derivative is clip(t,0,delta).

Take an ordinary block a_1,...,a_N in its physical order, alpha_j=a_j/2.
Construct r_j=(u_j,-y_j) backwards from r_N=(beta,-1). For alpha_j>0
choose the unique q_(j-1) in[0,1) satisfying

    (q_(j-1)+beta)/(p(q_(j-1))-beta)
       =max{beta/(C_beta-beta),
                  (y_j+(1-alpha_j)beta)/(u_j-(1-alpha_j)beta)},
    r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha_j*v(q_(j-1)).                              (3.3)

For alpha_j=0 keep the state and its projection. The denominator is
positive because u_j>=beta and alpha_j>0; the left ratio increases
from beta/(C_beta-beta) to infinity. We have u_j>=beta,y_j>=1 and
q_(j-1)<=q_j: at an interior current point the ratio is
(y_j+beta)/(u_j-beta), and subtracting alpha_j beta from its numerator
while adding it to its denominator can only decrease it. At q_j=1
the upper ratio is infinite. At q_j=0 the normal-cone condition
y_j<=beta(u_j-C_beta)/(C_beta-beta) keeps the next point capped.

For q_(j-1)>0, equation(3.3) places r_(j-1)-v(q_(j-1)) on its
outward circular normal; y_(j-1)>=1>=q_(j-1) fixes the sign. For
q_(j-1)=0 it gives y_(j-1)<=beta(u_(j-1)-C_beta)/(C_beta-beta),
the endpoint cone generated by the circular normal and(0,1).
The criterion <r-g,w-g><=0 for every w in K proves

    Pi_K(r_j)=v(q_j),
    u_0=beta+sum alpha_j*p(q_(j-1)),
    y_0=1+sum alpha_j*q_(j-1), u_0>=beta*y_0.                    (3.4)

This is a symbolic finite definition, not an executed recurrence.
For incoming ell>=0,D>0 put

    M=u_0+y_0/beta, sigma=D/M, ell_out=sigma*y_0,
    A=ell+ell_out/beta,
    Phi(X,Y)=E_(sigma K)(X-A,Y-ell_out).                          (3.5)

The local states(A+sigma*u_j,ell_out-sigma*y_j) start at(ell+D,0)
and follow exactly -(a_j/2)grad Phi. Inputs exceed ell and outputs
are in[0,ell_out). The final gradient is sigma(beta,-1). A selected
b>2 has T=b/2-1,D_out=sigma*T and exact post-checkpoint state

    (X^+,Y^+)=(A-beta*D_out,ell_out+D_out).                      (3.6)

The component is zero at this instant by(3.2); later motion may
reactivate it. It is zero whenever X<=ell,Y>=0. Its output derivative
vanishes for EVERY X when Y>=ell_out: replacing(p,-q) by(p,0) in
its maximization increases the objective by q(Y-ell_out)+q^2/2
for q>0. This exact isolation pays the active-coordinate induction.

## 4. Static function, all global updates, smoothness and exact radius

Apply Section3 to every block in(1.1), obtaining u_i,y_i,M_i. Set
ell_1=0,D_1=1 and

    T_i=b_(J+i-1)/2-1, sigma_i=D_i/M_i,
    ell_(i+1)=sigma_i*y_i, A_i=ell_i+ell_(i+1)/beta,
    D_(i+1)=sigma_i*T_i,
    Phi_i(X,Y)=E_(sigma_i K)(X-A_i,Y-ell_(i+1)).

Fix, before GD, the function on R^(k+1)

    delta=D_(k+1)/(1+S_(m-1)),
    F(x)=1/2 sum_(i=1)^k Phi_i(x_i,x_(i+1))
                 +1/2 Hub_delta(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1)), x_0=e_1.     (4.1)

The map stacking all coordinate pairs and the last scalar counts every
coordinate at most twice; its squared operator norm is at most2. Each
unscaled summand is convex1-smooth on its own coordinates. Thus the
factor1/2 makes F globally convex1-smooth. F>=0 and F(0)=0.

At ANY state define

    (P_i,-Q_i)=Pi_(sigma_i K)(x_i-A_i,x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1)),
    P_(k+1)=clip(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1),0,delta), Q_0=0.

The full physical update, retaining all earlier components, is

    x_j^+=x_j-(h/2)(P_j-Q_(j-1)), 1<=j<=k+1.                   (4.2)

At the start of block i its input is ell_i+D_i and later coordinates
are zero. The local states have x_i>=ell_i, so Q_(i-1)=0 by isolation.
The next component has input at most ell_(i+1) and output0 and hence
vanishes, as do later components. Components before i-1 involve neither
active coordinate. Thus(4.2) realizes EVERY ordinary step in Section3
and its checkpoint(3.6). Earlier components still follow(4.2) and may
have nonzero values and gradients. They are never reset to zero.

During the final suffix, if s' is the mass already read, the last excess
is exactly D_(k+1)-s'*delta/2>=delta: the preceding output derivative
Q_k is zero in this region and the scalar derivative is delta, proving
the assertion by induction. Consequently

    2F(x_n)=sum_(i=1)^k Phi_i(x_(n,i),x_(n,i+1))
                       +D_(k+1)^2/[2(1+S_(m-1))].             (4.3)

From(3.2) and nonnegativity the ENTIRE minimum set is

    C_min={x: x_i<=A_i, x_(i+1)>=beta(x_i-ell_i), 1<=i<=k;
                                                x_(k+1)<=ell_(k+1)}.

The point x_*=(A_1,ell_2,0,...,0) belongs to it. Equations(3.4) give
1-A_1=sigma_1*u_1, ell_2=beta*A_1=sigma_1*y_1, and
A_1<=1/(1+beta^2). A feasible point with x_1<0 has squared distance
at least1 from e_1. For 0<=x_1<=A_1 that square is at least
(1-x_1)^2+beta^2*x_1^2, decreasing on this interval and minimized
at A_1. The displayed x_* attains it. Thus the TRUE closest radius is

    R_m^2=(1-A_1)^2+ell_2^2=(u_1^2+y_1^2)/M_1^2.               (4.4)

All original directed interpolation rows hold for this static function:
the1-smooth descent bound for V(z)=F(z)-<grad F(y),z>, minimized at y,
gives F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>>=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.
Both orientations, minimizers and terminal points are included. Actual
Euclidean Gram matrices are positive semidefinite by definition.

## 5. Uniform local estimates when beta=1/k

Let w_j=(y_j+beta)/(q_j+beta). At an uncapped point the circular normal
says u_j-beta=(p(q_j)-beta)w_j. A cross product in the backward step
gives

    w_(j-1)=w_j[1+
       (p(q_(j-1))*q_j-q_(j-1)*p(q_j))
                     /(beta*(p(q_(j-1))+q_(j-1)))].             (5.1)

When q_(j-1)=0 this identity follows from y_(j-1)=y_j; if both q are
zero it is equality. Its numerator is the integral from q_(j-1) to q_j
of p(q_(j-1))-q_(j-1)*p'(t). Dividing by p(q_(j-1))+q_(j-1)
bounds the integrand by max(1,-p'(t)). Since w_N=1, multiplication
and 1+z<=exp(z) give

    w_0<=exp(I_beta/beta),
    I_beta=integral_0^1 max(1,-p'(t))dt
             =sqrt(2)(1+beta)-beta<2.

The integral is evaluated by splitting where -p'(t)=1, namely
t=(1+beta)/sqrt(2)-beta. Therefore

    y_0<=2exp(2k)=:Y_k.                                         (5.2)

At any uncapped point u_j<=beta+2k(Y_k+beta). Uncapped backward
steps form a contiguous portion, whose total u increment is at most
2k(Y_k+beta); their p is at least beta. Capped steps have p=C_beta.
It follows from(3.4) that

    u_0=beta+C_beta*s/2-E_gap,
    0<=E_gap<=4k^2(Y_k+beta),
    |M-C_beta*s/2|<=20k^2 exp(2k)=:K_k.                         (5.3)

The last bound pays beta,y_0/beta,E_gap; the loose constant20 works
for k>=4. A useful exact lower bound comes from the concave function
p(q)+q/beta. It is at least the smaller endpoint value C_beta, since
beta+1/beta>=C_beta for beta<=1/4. Thus

    M>=beta+1/beta+C_beta*s/2>=C_beta*(1+s/2).                   (5.4)

The mass recurrence S_(j+1)=2S_j+b_j has the exact solution

    d_m=c-2, v_mass=a_word/d_m in(0,1),
    1+S_j=v_mass*c^j+(1-v_mass)*2^j,
    b_j=d_m*S_j+c-1-d_m*(1-v_mass)*2^j.                        (5.5)

The inequality v_mass<1 follows from c(c-2)>1>eta_word. We have
v_mass->1 and c->lambda. Uniformly for J<=j<=m-1, S_j/(v_mass*c^j)
has error O(c^(-J)+(2/c)^J) from1. Furthermore

    k*K_k/S_J->0,
    S_J/lambda^J->exp(1/4),
    S_(m-1)/lambda^(m-1)->exp(1/4).                             (5.6)

The numerator in the first limit has logarithm O(k+log k), while the
denominator has logarithm J log(lambda)+O(1), and k/m->0. The other
limits follow from 0<=x-log(1+x)<=x^2/2 for x>=0 and j/m->1.
These are symbolic comparisons; no recurrence or numeric evaluation
was executed.

## 6. Amplitudes, cap ratios, and completion of the scale screen

The first transfer is T_1/M_1; later transfers use the one-level-shifted
gap in(1.1). Equations(5.3)--(5.6) give

    D_(k+1)=[d_m/C_beta]*[c*d_m/C_beta]^(k-1)*(1+o(1)).         (6.1)

The errors are summable: each normalization relative error is
O(K_k/S_J) and each mass or subtraction-of1 error is
O(c^(-J)+(2/c)^J); k times their sum tends to zero. Taking logarithms
proves the product assertion. Since c*d_m=1+O(1/m), its(k-1)st power
tends to1. Also log(beta+sqrt(1+2beta))/beta->2, so C_beta^k->exp(2).
Consequently

    D_(k+1)->(lambda-2)exp(-2),
    R_m^2->1, sigma_1~2/(C_beta*S_J).                           (6.2)

The radius conclusion uses u_1~C_beta*S_J/2,y_1<=Y_k and
M_1~C_beta*S_J/2. The next ordinary block after selected checkpoint i
is exactly H_(J+i-1), so uniformly for i<k,

    sigma_(i+1)/sigma_i=T_i/M_(i+1)->lambda-2.                  (6.3)

Eventually all these ratios are between1/3 and1/2. Hence

    sum_i sigma_i^2<=2sigma_1^2,
    B_(n_m)*sigma_1^2/R_m^2->0,                                (6.4)

the second quantity being O(lambda^(-m+2k)). Combining(6.2), the suffix
mass limit, and(1.2) proves(1.4). At the comparison point in Section2,
x_1<=A_1 gives the exact edge expression
Phi_1=Hub_((1+beta^2)sigma_1)(beta*x_1-x_2)/(1+beta^2), derived
also in Section7. At(0,-1) this equals the value in Section2 for large
m, while later inputs lie below their thresholds with nonnegative
outputs. This proves(2.2).

For reference T_infty<1. The j=0,b=1 enclosure of accepted
fact04219554e41d9750 gives Z_1<5, since B_1=G(1,1)=4 and
(2+3/100)^2<5. As exp(17/4)>16 and 2lambda>4, T_infty<5/64.
Its deficit from1 must be supplied by retained values for a benchmark
witness from this policy. The scale screen does not supply them.

## 7. The entire actual retired system and its two constraint residuals

Index the final suffix by t=0,...,N, N=2^(m-1)-1, with steps h_t
equal to the ordered H_(m-1). Write x(t) for its first k coordinates.
The incoming vector x(0) is the ACTUAL vector obtained from every
earlier update(4.2). After subsequent checkpoints, retired coordinates
may have changed in both directions. We do NOT set all of them to
their individual post-checkpoint values A_i-beta D_(i+1) at the root.

Throughout the suffix Q_k=0, and the fixed retired function is

    G_ret(x)=1/2 sum_(i<k) E_(sigma_i K)
                       (x_i-A_i,x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1))
                         +1/2 Hub_(C_beta*sigma_k)(x_k-A_k).    (7.1)

The last circular component is horizontal because its output is above
ell_(k+1). Its value is retained exactly, not dropped. The full retired
update is

    x_i(t+1)=x_i(t)-(h_t/2)[P_i(t)-Q_(i-1)(t)], 1<=i<=k,        (7.2)

where P_k is the derivative of the Huber in(7.1), and V_m=2G_ret(x(N)).
For i<k define

    r_i=x_i-A_i, z_i=beta(x_i-ell_i)-x_(i+1),
    U_i=P_i-beta Q_i>=0.

Also r_k=x_k-A_k,U_k=P_k,Q_0=Q_k=0. U_i is a horizontal cone
coefficient, distinct from the original benchmark U_n^ZJ. Since
beta A_i=beta ell_i+ell_(i+1), the local envelope is exactly

    Phi_i=max {U*r_i+Q*z_i-[(U+beta Q)^2+Q^2]/2:
            0<=Q<=sigma_i,
            0<=U<=sigma_i*p(Q/sigma_i)-beta Q}.                (7.3)

Indeed, the horizontal sections of K run from p=beta q to p=p(q).
The lower segment joins0 to(beta,-1), the upper boundary is concave,
and their convex hull contains every intervening point. Concavity also
bounds the convex hull above. Thus(7.3) is an identity.

When r_i<=0, maximizing first over U gives U_i=0, followed by

    Q_i=clip(z_i/(1+beta^2),0,sigma_i),
    Phi_i=Hub_((1+beta^2)sigma_i)(z_i)/(1+beta^2).               (7.4)

For general r_i, U_i can be positive. The exact residual updates are

    r_i^+=r_i-(h/2)(U_i+beta Q_i-Q_(i-1)),
    z_i^+=z_i-(h/2)[(1+beta^2)Q_i-beta Q_(i-1)-beta Q_(i+1)
                                               +beta U_i-U_(i+1)]. (7.5)

These include both neighboring contributions. Summing independent
two-Huber tail laws would delete genuine terms of(7.5).

## 8. Whole-hierarchy comparison with all input excursions charged

Put kappa=1+beta^2,theta=(1-beta)/(1+beta), and for i<k define

    qhat_i=clip(z_i/kappa,0,sigma_i), qhat_0=qhat_k=0,
    barsigma_i=sigma_i for 1<=i<k, barsigma_0=barsigma_k=0,
    e_i=beta*(barsigma_(i-1)+barsigma_(i+1)),
    gamma_i=kappa*sigma_i-e_i.

Here e_i,gamma_i are fixed cap combinations. From(6.3) and beta->0,
eventually gamma_i>0 and, uniformly for i<k,

    d_m*e_i<=gamma_i.                                         (8.1)

Every exact maximizer in(7.3) satisfies

    Q_i<=qhat_i,
    kappa(qhat_i-Q_i)-beta U_i<=theta U_i.                     (8.2)

For the complete curved-regime check, the function
A(q)=sigma_i*p(q/sigma_i)-beta q decreases continuously from
C_beta*sigma_i to0. At fixed U its feasible Q interval is[0,qmax(U)],
where A(qmax(U))=U and qmax(0)=sigma_i. The maximizer therefore has
Q=clip((z-beta U)/kappa,0,qmax(U)), proving Q<=qhat. If the upper
cap is inactive, kappa(qhat-Q)<=beta U. If active, Q=qmax(U).
Concavity and endpoint values imply p(q)+q>=1+beta, hence
U=A(Q)>=(1+beta)(sigma_i-Q). It follows that
kappa(qhat-Q)-beta U<=kappa(sigma_i-Q)-beta U<=theta U.
Lower and upper cap boundaries are included.

Insert(8.2) into(7.5). All neighbor differences that are dropped are
NONPOSITIVE, since Q_j<=qhat_j. The resulting unconditional step bound is

    z_i^+<=z_i-(h/2)[kappa qhat_i-beta qhat_(i-1)-beta qhat_(i+1)]
                                  +(h/2)[theta U_i+U_(i+1)].  (8.3)

Define the nonnegative charge from the ACTUAL suffix trajectory

    L_i=(1/2)sum_(t=0)^(N-1) h_t[theta U_i(t)+U_(i+1)(t)].     (8.4)

For any starting vector, the final residual obeys

    z_i(N)<=max{kappa*sigma_i+(c-1)e_i/2,
                              z_i(0)-gamma_i*S_(m-1)/2}+L_i.  (8.5)

If z_i(N)<=kappa*sigma_i this is immediate. Otherwise take its LAST
entry into z_i>kappa*sigma_i. If it was above the threshold at all
samples, qhat_i=sigma_i throughout, and summing(8.3) with the neighbor
caps gives the second term plus L_i. Otherwise the last entry uses an
actual step b_j,0<=j<m-1, whose preceding z_i is at most kappa*sigma_i.
Dropping only its nonpositive own term bounds the new residual by
kappa*sigma_i+b_j e_i/2 plus that step's charge. Immediately after
this occurrence is a complete H_j within the remaining suffix: this
right-subtree property follows inductively from the word definition.
At all samples after the last entry, qhat_i=sigma_i. The following
H_j therefore loses at least gamma_i*S_j/2 apart from its charges;
all further steps lose a nonnegative amount apart from their charges.
The uncharged bound is kappa*sigma_i+(b_j e_i-gamma_i S_j)/2.
Using(5.5), b_j<=d_m S_j+c-1, and(8.1) proves the first term.
Adding at most L_i pays EVERY source, including the crossing and all
large steps. This is an argument on the ordered hierarchy, not a
mass-only replacement of its nonlinear dynamics.

For this policy's ACTUAL incoming vector the second term in(8.5) is
nonpositive for all sufficiently large m. Let S_pre=S_(m-1)+b_(m-1),
the complete mass before the suffix. Global updates(4.2) and caps give

    delta_(i1)-C_beta*sigma_i*S_pre/2<=x_i(0)
                    <=delta_(i1)+sigma_(i-1)*S_pre/2,

where sigma_0=0 and delta_(i1) is1 for i=1 and0 otherwise. Therefore

    z_i(0)<=beta*delta_(i1)
                   +(beta*sigma_(i-1)+C_beta*sigma_(i+1))*S_pre/2. (8.6)

Only the nonpositive -beta ell_i was dropped. Uniformly in i, the
coefficient divided by sigma_i tends to lambda-2,
S_pre/S_(m-1)->lambda-1, and gamma_i/sigma_i->1. Their product
tends to (lambda-2)(lambda-1)=2-sqrt(2)<1. For i=1 the extra ratio
beta/(sigma_1*S_(m-1)) tends to0 by(6.2) and J=m-k. This proves
the incoming budget. As kappa+(c-1)e_i/(2sigma_i)->1 uniformly,
(8.5) yields eventually

    (z_i(N))_+<=2sigma_i+L_i.                                 (8.7)

There is no assumption that earlier components were zero at the root.
The exact maximization formula also gives

    Phi_i<=C_beta*sigma_i*(r_i)_++sigma_i*(z_i)_+, i<k,
    Phi_k<=C_beta*sigma_k*(r_k)_+.

Define one remaining nonnegative quantity on the ACTUAL trajectory:

    X_m=C_beta*sum_(i=1)^k sigma_i*(r_i(N))_+
           +(1/2)sum_(t=0)^(N-1) h_t
                         sum_(i=1)^(k-1) sigma_i
                                      [theta U_i(t)+U_(i+1)(t)]. (8.8)

Summing(8.7) and using(6.4) proves the unconditional comparison

    0<=V_m<=4sigma_1^2+X_m,
    B_(n_m)E_m<=T_m+o(1)+B_(n_m)X_m/R_m^2.                    (8.9)

Every old value is included. X_m has NOT been proved small. It is a
sufficient interface, not an equivalent formula for V_m: accumulated
nonnegative charges can overcount activity dissipated later. Proving
B_(n_m)X_m/R_m^2->0 would imply the complete scoped obstruction
B_(n_m)E_m->T_infty<1. No such estimate is asserted here.

## 9. The conditional slab result and a counterexample to blanket invariance

If every suffix sample satisfied x_i(t)<=A_i for all i<=k, then
U_i(t)=0 by(7.3)--(7.4), and every terminal(r_i)_+=0. Thus X_m=0
and(8.9) proves a full asymptotic obstruction. This is CONDITIONAL,
not a property proved for the actual trajectory. It already concerns
the coupled edge system, with no independence assumption.

The entire slab {x_i<=A_i for 1<=i<=k} is NOT invariant for this
same static function and the available large steps. For all large m,
consider this suffix state in the original R^(k+1):

    x_1=A_1, x_2=ell_2-kappa*sigma_1, x_3=ell_3,
    x_4=...=x_k=0, x_(k+1)=ell_(k+1)+D_(k+1).

All retired inputs are at most their A_i. The first component has
z_1=kappa*sigma_1 and(P_1,Q_1)=(beta*sigma_1,sigma_1).
The second has input<A_2 and output ell_3, so P_2=Q_2=0. Later
circular components vanish: the third input is its threshold ell_3
and its output0; subsequent inputs are0 with nonnegative outputs.
The terminal scalar does not affect coordinate2. Therefore

    x_2^+=ell_2-kappa*sigma_1+h*sigma_1/2>A_2
       whenever h>2kappa+2ell_3/(beta*sigma_1).                 (9.1)

The unchanged suffix contains h=b_(m-2). Meanwhile
ell_3/(beta*sigma_1)=T_1*y_2/(beta*M_2)=O(k exp(2k)) by
(5.2),(5.4),(5.5), whereas b_(m-2) grows like lambda^(m-2).
Thus(9.1) holds eventually. This uses the SAME function and a physical
step in the suffix; it is not a construction repair.

The falsifying state is NOT asserted to be the actual state at that
step. It refutes blanket slab invariance only. A trajectory-specific
barrier or a bound on the excursion flux could still hold. The example
identifies why a two-checkpoint absorption argument cannot be applied
independently to every pair.

## 10. Exact energy accounting and the remaining interfaces

An exact identity also keeps the signed effects that the sufficient
positive-source bound(8.8) may overcount. Let z_*=(A_1,ell_2,0,...,0)
in R^k, which minimizes G_ret, and define at each suffix sample

    d_i=P_i-Q_(i-1),
    N_grad=sum_i P_i^2+sum_(i<k)Q_i^2,
    C_cross=sum_(i=2)^k P_i Q_(i-1),
    slack=sum_(i<k)[-<(P_i,-Q_i),
                        (z_*(i)-A_i,z_*(i+1)-ell_(i+1))>]
                                     -P_k*(z_*(k)-A_k)>=0.

The slack sign follows from the polar-cone condition at a minimizer;
it is not an assumed sign for individual coordinates. The maximization
identity gives <grad G_ret(x),x-z_*>=G_ret(x)+N_grad/4+slack/2.
Since sum_i d_i^2=N_grad-2C_cross, expansion of(7.2) gives for EVERY
physical step, with no restriction on its size,

    ||x-z_*||^2-||x^+-z_*||^2
       =2h G_ret(x)+h(2-h)N_grad/4
                         +h^2 C_cross/2+h*slack.              (10.1)

C_cross and slack are nonnegative. The N_grad coefficient is negative
when h>2 and MUST be paid. Dropping it would not be a descent proof.

For the exact terminal value, write
D_E(v,u)=E(v)-E(u)-<grad E(u),v-u>>=0 for the Bregman remainder of
a differentiable convex E. For i<k take the fixed envelope E_(sigma_i K)
at its two centered coordinates, and for i=k take Hub_(C_beta*sigma_k)
at r_k. Let B_i(t) denote the remainder from sample t to t+1. Direct
first-order expansion of(7.1) gives the equality

    V_m=2G_ret(x(0))
              -(1/2)sum_t h_t sum_i d_i(t)^2
                                      +sum_t sum_i B_i(t).    (10.2)

All overlaps enter through d_i, and all overshoots remain in the actual
Bregman terms. Neither sum has been compared sharply enough here.

A sufficient retained contribution for a diagnostic lower witness at
the original benchmark is, for example,

    liminf_m B_(n_m)*V_m/R_m^2>=1-T_infty.                     (10.3)

Its exact V_m is specified by the actual static trajectory or by(10.2).
A strict version would give a strict margin for THIS diagnostic family.
A strict upper bound for the same term below1-T_infty would instead
obstruct this policy. The explicit sufficient unpaid obstruction
inequality supplied by the new comparison is

    B_(n_m)*X_m/R_m^2 ->0.                                   (10.4)

Attempt one: obtain(10.4) from slab invariance. Section9 refutes general
invariance; actual-trajectory invariance remains unproved. Attempt two:
use distance descent. Equation(10.1) exposes the unpaid h(2-h) term.
Its favorable coupled cross term is exact, but no comparison paying
all large-step negative terms from it has been established.

No snapshot, independent pair sum, or terminal-only product is offered
as the result. Equations(1.3),(8.8)--(8.9),(10.2)--(10.4) state exactly
what is paid and what is not. This round does NOT complete a retained
terminal bound for the chosen policy.

Even a successful(10.3) only treats H_(m,1/4) at horizons2^m-1.
The original all-word/all-horizon minimax question would still require
a lower bound for W_n(h) uniform over EVERY nonnegative word h of
length n, at the original B_n scale, or another valid route to the
original target. No such extension follows from this one policy.

## 11. Retrieval, resources, and handoff

Query-memory recalled the shifted-circle construction, complete
two-checkpoint draft, scoped scalar/tree failures, and current guidance.
Fact searches found no accepted growing circular-prefix result.
Fact5fd396eba75651e7 concerns different spiral families and is not a
premise. Only the original-ray conclusion of04219554e41d9750 is used;
its statement and relevant proof were read.

Matlas retrieval for overlapping projection envelopes and long-step
retained Huber transport returned three non-applicable results:
2011.14269 Lemma3.6 is a continuous-flow perturbation bound;
2212.04459 Lemma3 is a proximal descent inequality;2012.15458 Lemma2.1
concerns a different Moreau composition. None was imported.

The exact primary source was downloaded and extracted:
Kaizhao Liu and Yuhan Ye, Silver Rate Is (Almost) Optimal for Gradient
Descent: The Strongly Convex Case, arXiv:2609.26873v1,
https://arxiv.org/html/2609.26873v1. Read the preceding-component
updates, FactA.1, and the circular construction and checkpoint proof.
Its finite-condition-number contraction and relative-function
normalization do not apply here. The circular envelope was reconstructed
directly without a quadratic term, with overlap smoothness and closest
radius derived anew. No external theorem is used as a black box.

Skills: query-memory, obtain-immediate-conclusions, search-math-results,
direct-proving, construct-counterexamples. TASK and current guidance
explicitly allocate no formal gate, so fact_submit was not called.
All new assertions remain one joint unverified proof attempt.

Mathematical programs0; numerical evaluations0; arithmetic replays0;
executed recurrences0; solvers0; benchmark DP0; formal submissions0;
new accepted facts0; installations0; consults0; subagents0; repairs0.
Only administrative file I/O, text recall, source retrieval, HTML text

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002840 -->
# Actual retained values: two endpoints paid, the middle remains open

Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED ANALYTICAL PROOF ATTEMPT, with a quantitative
partial terminal estimate. No formal gate was allocated or called. This
report does not establish a complete obstruction or a benchmark witness
for the frozen policy, and does not resolve the original minimax problem.

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; worker max3; completed baseline60;
one round, completed ceiling61 / attempted ceiling62. TASK.md and latest
read guidance56f0c49630564f24 apply. Plan finding9569f5e8ea1c9a9e.
The full evidence84a4435a88a9f487 and a9423b621329882d was read. Those
drafts are provenance, not accepted premises. The construction and all
estimates used below are derived jointly here. The only accepted input
is the benchmark/ray conclusion of fact04219554e41d9750.

The new partial conclusion is that the FIRST and LAST actual circular
component values have vanishing original-benchmark normalization. Both
proofs use the actual ordered suffix, all preceding physical updates,
and state-dependent signs. They do not require the accumulated positive
charge X_m to be small. The middle k-2 components remain unpaid.

## 1. The unchanged word, function, and initialization

For integers m>=16 set

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), k=floor(sqrt(m)), J=m-k, beta=1/k,
    eta_word=1-1/(4m), c=lambda*(1+1/(4m)), a_word=eta_word/c,
    d=c-2, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), b_j=1+a_word*c^j,
    S_j=sum H_j, n_m=2^m-1.

All children use the SAME enclosing m. Select precisely the first
occurrences of levels J,...,m-1, at zero-based positions
2^(J+i-1)-1, i=1,...,k. The word is

    (H_J,b_J,H_J,b_(J+1),H_(J+1),...,b_(m-1),H_(m-1)).       (1.1)

Let s_1=S_J, s_i=S_(J+i-2) for 2<=i<=k, and s_(k+1)=S_(m-1).
Selected step i is b_(J+i-1). No extra checkpoint, step deletion,
reordering, state reset, or modification of the function is made.

For 0<=q<=1 define

    p(q)=beta+sqrt((1+beta)^2-(q+beta)^2),
    C_beta=beta+sqrt(1+2beta), v(q)=(p(q),-q),
    K=conv({0} union {v(q):0<=q<=1}).

For a compact convex body C containing0 put

    E_C(w)=max_(g in C){<g,w>-||g||^2/2}.

The one-sided Huber function Hub_delta is0 on(-infinity,0], t^2/2
on[0,delta], and delta*t-delta^2/2 on[delta,infinity).

Here is the exact symbolic local definition, included to fix the SAME
static function. For each ordinary block a_1,...,a_N in its physical
order, put alpha_j=a_j/2. Starting from (u_N,-y_N)=(beta,-1), choose
q_(j-1) in[0,1) backwards by

    (q_(j-1)+beta)/(p(q_(j-1))-beta)
      =max{beta/(C_beta-beta),
               (y_j+(1-alpha_j)*beta)/(u_j-(1-alpha_j)*beta)},
    (u_(j-1),-y_(j-1))=(u_j,-y_j)+alpha_j*v(q_(j-1)).         (1.2)

The word has strictly positive steps. The ratio on the left increases
from beta/(C_beta-beta) to infinity, and the denominator on the right
is positive. The formula therefore uniquely specifies a finite set of
local data without executing a recurrence. Denote the starting local
coordinates for block i by u_i,y_i and put M_i=u_i+y_i/beta.

Set ell_1=0,D_1=1 and recursively define static constants

    T_i=b_(J+i-1)/2-1, sigma_i=D_i/M_i,
    ell_(i+1)=sigma_i*y_i, A_i=ell_i+ell_(i+1)/beta,
    D_(i+1)=sigma_i*T_i,
    Phi_i(X,Y)=E_(sigma_i K)(X-A_i,Y-ell_(i+1)).              (1.3)

For sufficiently large m all T_i are positive. The fixed function and
initial point are

    delta=D_(k+1)/(1+S_(m-1)),
    F(x)=1/2 sum_(i=1)^k Phi_i(x_i,x_(i+1))
                  +1/2 Hub_delta(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1)),
    x_0=e_1 in R^(k+1).                                    (1.4)

The physical update is x^+=x-h grad F(x). Multiplying F by an arbitrary
advertised L>0 and dividing the physical gradient step by L gives the
same trajectory and normalized objective.

## 2. Geometry, chronology, and true radius, derived jointly

Completing the square shows grad E_C(w)=Pi_C(w), the Euclidean
projection onto C. Projection optimality at w and w' gives
||Pi_C(w)-Pi_C(w')||^2<=<Pi_C(w)-Pi_C(w'),w-w'>. Comparing the two
maximization formulas gives differentiability with this gradient.
Thus E_C is convex and1-smooth. It is nonnegative, and is zero exactly
on the polar cone of C: a positive support value remains positive in
the envelope at a sufficiently small positive multiple of the
supporting vector.

The horizontal sections of K are

    0<=q<=1, beta*q<=p<=p(q).                               (2.1)

The lower boundary is the segment joining0 to(beta,-1), and the
concave upper boundary is the displayed circular arc. The endpoints
are(C_beta,0),(beta,-1). In particular (p,0) belongs to K whenever
(p,-q) does. Its cone has those two extreme rays. Therefore

    E_(sigma K)(U,V)=0 iff U<=0 and beta*U-V<=0.              (2.2)

The disk center(beta,beta), radius1+beta contains0. Its outward radial
vector at(p(q),-q) supports K. In (1.2), u_j>=beta,y_j>=1 and the
q's are nondecreasing in forward order. Indeed, before a backward
step the uncapped supporting ratio is(y_j+beta)/(u_j-beta); the
ratio defining its predecessor reduces the numerator by alpha_j*beta
and increases the denominator by the same amount. At q=0 the capped
normal-cone inequality is y_j<=beta*(u_j-C_beta)/(C_beta-beta),
which is preserved by further capped backward steps. Formula(1.2)
puts the vector from the candidate projection to the new point on
the circular outward normal, or in the endpoint normal cone when
q=0. Projection optimality gives

    Pi_K(u_j,-y_j)=v(q_j),
    u_i=beta+sum_j alpha_j*p(q_(j-1)),
    y_i=1+sum_j alpha_j*q_(j-1), u_i>=beta*y_i.              (2.3)

The local states(A_i+sigma_i*u_j,ell_(i+1)-sigma_i*y_j)
start at(ell_i+D_i,0), follow every step -(a_j/2)grad Phi_i,
and have last gradient sigma_i*(beta,-1). The checkpoint produces

    (x_i,x_(i+1))=(A_i-beta*D_(i+1),ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1)).     (2.4)

At that instant Phi_i is zero by(2.2), but it may reactivate later.
The construction does not reset it.

For EVERY state write

    (P_i,-Q_i)=Pi_(sigma_i K)(x_i-A_i,x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1)),
    P_(k+1)=clip(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1),0,delta), Q_0=0.

Then every physical update, including all prehistory, is

    x_i^+=x_i-(h/2)(P_i-Q_(i-1)), 1<=i<=k+1.              (2.5)

If x_(i+1)>=ell_(i+1), replacing(p,-q) by(p,0) improves the
projection objective by q*(x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1))+q^2/2 for q>0.
Consequently Q_i=0 in that region. If x_i<=ell_i and the output
is nonnegative, Phi_i is zero by(2.2). These two properties prove
inductively that the designated active pair follows its local states:
the previous component contributes no derivative in its input
coordinate; the next component and all subsequent components vanish.
Every earlier retired coordinate still follows(2.5). This proves the
chronology without substituting post-checkpoint snapshots for the
actual later states.

The map stacking adjacent pairs and the last coordinate has squared
operator norm at most2. The factor1/2 in(1.4) therefore makes F
globally convex1-smooth. Also F>=0 and F(0)=0. Its complete minimum
set is

    C_min={x: x_i<=A_i, x_(i+1)>=beta*(x_i-ell_i), 1<=i<=k;
                                         x_(k+1)<=ell_(k+1)}.

The point x_*=(A_1,ell_2,0,...,0) is feasible. Since
1-A_1=sigma_1*u_1, ell_2=beta*A_1=sigma_1*y_1 and
u_1>=beta*y_1, A_1<=1/(1+beta^2). For a feasible point with
x_1<0 the squared distance from e_1 is at least1. On[0,A_1] the
lower bound (1-x_1)^2+beta^2*x_1^2 decreases up to A_1 and is
attained by x_*. Thus the true closest radius is

    R_m^2=dist(e_1,C_min)^2
         =(1-A_1)^2+ell_2^2=(u_1^2+y_1^2)/M_1^2.            (2.6)

Convex1-smooth interpolation, in both directions and including all
samples and minimizers, follows directly: apply the smooth descent
inequality to F(z)-<grad F(y),z>, whose minimum occurs at y, to get
F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>>=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.
All trajectory Gram matrices are actual Euclidean Gram matrices.

## 3. Uniform scales and the accepted benchmark input

We give the local estimates because earlier unverified drafts are not
premises. In a backward block let w_j=(y_j+beta)/(q_j+beta).
At an uncapped point u_j-beta=(p(q_j)-beta)*w_j. A cross product
in(1.2) gives

    w_(j-1)=w_j*(1+
      [p(q_(j-1))*q_j-q_(j-1)*p(q_j)]
                  /[beta*(p(q_(j-1))+q_(j-1))]).             (3.1)

At a transition to q_(j-1)=0 the same identity follows from
y_(j-1)=y_j, and if both q's are zero it is equality. Its numerator
is the integral of p(q_(j-1))-q_(j-1)*p'(t) between the two q's.
After division by p(q_(j-1))+q_(j-1), this integrand is at most
max(1,-p'(t)). Multiplication of(3.1), w_N=1 and 1+z<=exp(z)
therefore yield

    y_i<=2 exp(2k)=:Y_k,
    integral_0^1 max(1,-p'(t))dt=sqrt(2)*(1+beta)-beta<2.     (3.2)

For the integral, split at t=(1+beta)/sqrt(2)-beta and integrate
-p' on the second interval. At an uncapped point,
u_j<=beta+2k*(Y_k+beta). Such backward steps form one contiguous
portion; their total u increment is at most2k*(Y_k+beta), and
their p is at least beta. The remaining steps have p=C_beta.
Thus their total deficit from C_beta times half the block mass is
at most4k^2*(Y_k+beta). Combining this with(2.3) and y_i/beta
gives, uniformly in i,

    |M_i-C_beta*s_i/2|<=K_k, K_k=20k^2 exp(2k).              (3.3)

The concave function p(q)+q/beta has smaller endpoint C_beta.
Consequently (2.3) also gives the useful exact lower bound

    M_i>=beta+1/beta+C_beta*s_i/2
        >=C_beta*(1+s_i/2).                                (3.4)

Put v_mass=a_word/d. One has0<v_mass<1, since c*(c-2)>1>eta_word.
Solving S_(j+1)=2S_j+b_j by substitution gives the exact identities

    1+S_j=v_mass*c^j+(1-v_mass)*2^j,
    b_j=d*S_j+c-1-d*(1-v_mass)*2^j.                         (3.5)

They imply uniformly for J<=j<=m-1 that S_j/(v_mass*c^j)->1,
k*K_k/S_J->0, and

    S_J/lambda^J->exp(1/4),
    S_(m-r)/lambda^(m-r)->exp(1/4) for fixed r>=1.           (3.6)

For these claims, the relative error in the first limit is bounded
by a constant times c^(-J)+(2/c)^J. The logarithm of k*K_k is
O(k+log k), whereas J log(lambda) is of order m. Finally use
0<=x-log(1+x)<=x^2/2 with x=1/(4m), and k/m->0.

The first transfer ratio T_1/M_1 equals(d/C_beta)*(1+o(1)); all
later ones equal(c*d/C_beta)*(1+o(1)). Their relative errors are
uniformly O(K_k/S_J+c^(-J)+(2/c)^J), and k times this bound
tends to zero. Thus

    D_(k+1)=(d/C_beta)*(c*d/C_beta)^(k-1)*(1+o(1))
            ->(lambda-2)*exp(-2),
    R_m^2->1, sigma_1~2/(C_beta*S_J).                        (3.7)

Here c*d=1+O(1/m), and log(C_beta)/beta->2, so C_beta^k->exp(2).
The radius follows from(2.6),(3.2),(3.3). For every i<k,

    sigma_(i+1)/sigma_i=T_i/M_(i+1)->lambda-2               (3.8)

uniformly. Eventually all these ratios lie in[1/3,1/2], so
sum_i sigma_i^2<=2sigma_1^2.

The ORIGINAL all-split benchmark is C_0=B_0=1 and

    C_n=max_(0<=r<n) Omega(C_r,C_(n-r-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=r<n) G(C_r,B_(n-r-1)),
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2.

Only fact04219554e41d9750 is used for

    0<Z_1=lim_m lambda^(-m)*B_(2^m-1)<infinity.              (3.9)

Its statement and proof Section7 were read. Its enclosure at b=1,j=0
also gives Z_1<5 from B_1=4. No seed assumption or unverified
construction is imported from that fact.

A growth estimate needed below is

    (B_(n_m)/R_m^2)*sigma_1^2*(1+K_k+2^m)->0.              (3.10)

Indeed B*sigma_1^2=O(lambda^(-m+2k)). Multiplication by K_k is
still exponentially decreasing in m because k=O(sqrt(m)); the term
2^m gives O((2/lambda)^m*lambda^(2k)), which tends to zero since
lambda>2. No numerical evaluation or recurrence replay is used.

## 4. Actual retired dynamics and screen I: the accumulated charge

Index the actual final suffix H_(m-1) by t=0,...,N,
N=2^(m-1)-1. Its input x(0) is the result of EVERY earlier step(2.5).
The last excess evolves as D_(k+1)-delta times half the mass already
read. It remains at least delta, and Q_k=0 by the output-isolation
property. The fixed function governing the first k coordinates is

    G_ret(x)=1/2 sum_(i<k) Phi_i(x_i,x_(i+1))
                     +1/2 Hub_(C_beta*sigma_k)(x_k-A_k).    (4.1)

The retired update is still(2.5), with Q_0=Q_k=0, and
P_k=clip(x_k-A_k,0,C_beta*sigma_k). Write

    r_i=x_i-A_i,
    z_i=beta*(x_i-ell_i)-x_(i+1), i<k,
    U_i=P_i-beta*Q_i for i<k, U_k=P_k,
    kappa=1+beta^2, theta=(1-beta)/(1+beta).

In the cone coordinates P=U+beta*Q, the exact maximization formula is

    Phi_i=max {U*r_i+Q*z_i-[(U+beta*Q)^2+Q^2]/2:
      0<=Q<=sigma_i,
      0<=U<=sigma_i*p(Q/sigma_i)-beta*Q}.                    (4.2)

Hence U_i>=0. If r_i<=0 then U_i=0 and

    Q_i=clip(z_i/kappa,0,sigma_i),
    Phi_i=Hub_(kappa*sigma_i)(z_i)/kappa.                    (4.3)

There are also the unconditional complementary sign facts

    U_i>0 implies x_i>A_i>=ell_i and Q_(i-1)=0;
    Q_i>0 implies x_(i+1)<ell_(i+1) and U_(i+1)=0.          (4.4)

The exact residual equations, including BOTH neighbors, are

    r_i^+=r_i-(h/2)(U_i+beta*Q_i-Q_(i-1)),
    z_i^+=z_i-(h/2)[kappa*Q_i-beta*Q_(i-1)-beta*Q_(i+1)
                                             +beta*U_i-U_(i+1)]. (4.5)

The retained value and the total normalized payoff are exactly

    V_m=2G_ret(x(N))=sum_(i=1)^k Phi_i(x(N)),
    B_(n_m)*2F(x_(n_m))/R_m^2=T_m+B_(n_m)*V_m/R_m^2,
    T_m=B_(n_m)*D_(k+1)^2/[2(1+S_(m-1))*R_m^2]
        ->T_infty=Z_1*exp(-17/4)/(2lambda).                 (4.6)

In the sum, Phi_k means exactly the last horizontal envelope, equal
to the original circular value at the actual state. The limit follows
from(3.6)--(3.9) and (lambda-2)^2=lambda^(-2). In particular
T_infty<5/64<1, using exp(17/4)>16 and2lambda>4.

Screen I assesses the previous sufficient NONNEGATIVE charge

    X_m=C_beta*sum_i sigma_i*(r_i(N))_+
         +(1/2)sum_t h_t sum_(i<k) sigma_i
                                     [theta*U_i(t)+U_(i+1)(t)]. (4.7)

The elementary cap estimate for its flux part is only

    X_flux <= (C_beta*S_(m-1)/2)
                   sum_(i<k) sigma_i*(theta*sigma_i+sigma_(i+1)).

The RIGHT SIDE after normalization has order lambda^(2k), by the
positive limiting ratio sigma_2/sigma_1 and(3.6)--(3.9). It does not
vanish or supply the required strict constant. This is a failure of
that upper comparison; it is NOT a lower bound on actual X_m.

Signed coordinate budgets do not automatically improve it. Define
H_i^U=(1/2)sum_t h_t U_i(t) and H_i^Q=(1/2)sum_t h_t Q_i(t),
with H_0^Q=H_k^Q=0. Then

    x_i(0)-x_i(N)=H_i^U+beta*H_i^Q-H_(i-1)^Q.              (4.8)

The telescoping positive weights that eliminate every H_i^Q are
proportional to beta^(i-1), since their adjacent ratio must be beta.
The bulk weights in(4.7) have adjacent ratios asymptotic to lambda-2,
with different harmless endpoint factors. Explicitly their coefficients
on H_i^U are theta*sigma_1, theta*sigma_i+sigma_(i-1) for
2<=i<k, and sigma_(k-1) at i=k. They are not those telescoping
weights. Converting between the two
can cost ((lambda-2)/beta)^O(k), and the signed endpoint displacement
has not itself been bounded on the required scale.

The crucial actual restriction is stronger than this budget for the
first coordinate. At its own checkpoint x_1=A_1-beta*D_2. Thereafter
x_1 never increases, since Q_0=0 and P_1>=0 in EVERY physical step.
Thus throughout the suffix

    r_1<=-beta*D_2<0, U_1=0.                               (4.9)

This is a property of the initialized trajectory, not invariance of
the entire input slab. Even after dropping U_1 from the cap estimate,
the sigma_1*U_2 charge retains the same lambda^(2k) scale in that
upper bound. The actual barrier will remove the U_2 charge from the FIRST
terminal component by a last-entry argument. We have not proved
that X_m itself fails to vanish, nor supplied a lower bound showing
that it overcounts by a nonvanishing amount. Its status remains open.
Only the unnecessary use of all-time U_2 to bound Phi_1 is eliminated.

## 5. Screen II: quantitative actual terminal bounds at both ends

This is the viable part of the round. A last entry uses an occurrence
of b_j in H_(m-1). Immediately following that occurrence lies an entire
H_j inside the remaining suffix. This property follows by induction
from H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), and keeps the original order and enclosing
m. It is used only on samples AFTER the last entry, so the claimed
region persists by the choice of the last entry, not by an assumed
invariance theorem.

### 5.1 First circular component: the apparent source switches off

By(4.9), formula(4.3) applies to component1 at EVERY suffix sample.
The exact scalar residual update is

    z_1^+=z_1-(h/2)(kappa*Q_1-P_2).                        (5.1)

It includes the full P_2=U_2+beta*Q_2. If z_1>kappa*sigma_1,
then Q_1=sigma_1 and

    x_2=beta*x_1-z_1
       <ell_2-beta^2*D_2-kappa*sigma_1<ell_2.

Therefore r_2<0, U_2=0 and P_2=beta*Q_2<=beta*sigma_2.
Define gamma_1=kappa*sigma_1-beta*sigma_2. In this region every
physical step loses at least h*gamma_1/2 in z_1, with no restriction
on h. In contrast, an entry step from z_1<=kappa*sigma_1 can add
at most h*C_beta*sigma_2/2. These are different, justified caps.

Eventually gamma_1>0 and

    d*C_beta*sigma_2<=gamma_1,                              (5.2)

because the ratio of the left side to sigma_1 tends to
(lambda-2)^2<1, whereas gamma_1/sigma_1 tends to1.

If z_1(N)>kappa*sigma_1 and there was an entry during the suffix,
take its last occurrence, at b_j. All later samples remain above the
threshold. Pay the entry with C_beta*sigma_2 and the following H_j
with gamma_1. Every subsequent step has nonnegative loss. Thus

    z_1(N)<=kappa*sigma_1
                      +(b_j*C_beta*sigma_2-gamma_1*S_j)/2
            <=kappa*sigma_1+(c-1)*C_beta*sigma_2/2,         (5.3)

where(3.5) and(5.2) pay every large bridge.

The case of no entry must use the ACTUAL full prehistory. Let
S_pre=S_(m-1)+b_(m-1) be the mass before the final suffix. From
(2.5), x_1(0)<=1 and x_2(0)>=-C_beta*sigma_2*S_pre/2.
Hence

    z_1(0)<=beta+C_beta*sigma_2*S_pre/2.                    (5.4)

If all samples from t=0 were above the threshold, summing(5.1)
would give z_1(N)<=z_1(0)-gamma_1*S_(m-1)/2. This is negative
eventually: S_pre/S_(m-1)->lambda-1 and

    C_beta*sigma_2*S_pre/(gamma_1*S_(m-1))
                  ->(lambda-2)*(lambda-1)=2-sqrt(2)<1,
    beta/(sigma_1*S_(m-1))->0.

So the no-entry case cannot leave a terminal positive residual above
the threshold. If the terminal residual is below the threshold, (5.3)
already bounds its positive part. Consequently, for every sufficiently
large m, on the prescribed actual trajectory,

    (z_1(N))_+<=kappa*sigma_1+(c-1)*C_beta*sigma_2/2
               <=2sigma_1,
    0<=Phi_1(x(N))<=2sigma_1^2.                             (5.5)

This is a full terminal bound for that component. No U_2 integral,
independent two-Huber trajectory, or alternative state was substituted.
By(3.10), B_(n_m)*Phi_1(x(N))/R_m^2 tends to zero.

### 5.2 Last circular component: the small cap mismatch is paid

Write L_k=C_beta*sigma_k for the horizontal gradient cap. The actual
checkpoint formula for the active kth coordinate gives

    r_k(0)=-beta*D_(k+1)<0.                                (5.6)

This statement concerns only coordinate k at its OWN checkpoint; it
makes no assertion about the other retired coordinates. During its
active ordinary block and checkpoint Q_(k-1)=0 by output isolation,
so preceding gradients do not alter this particular formula.

At any suffix sample with r_k>L_k, x_k>ell_k, whence Q_(k-1)=0
and P_k=L_k. Thus r_k loses h*L_k/2 there. At an entry from
r_k<=L_k its increase is at most h*sigma_(k-1)/2. If the terminal
residual exceeds L_k, (5.6) guarantees an entry. Its last occurrence
at b_j and its following H_j give

    (r_k(N))_+<=L_k+(1/2) max_(0<=j<=m-2)
                                      [b_j*sigma_(k-1)-L_k*S_j]_+.
                                                                  (5.7)

No assumption of distance descent is used. Put s=S_(m-2),
T=b_(m-2)/2-1 and Delta_k=d-C_beta*T/M_k. The EXACT ratio
sigma_k/sigma_(k-1)=T/M_k follows from(1.3). Thus(3.5) implies

    (r_k(N))_+<=L_k+(sigma_(k-1)/2)
                                      [c-1+(Delta_k)_+*s]. (5.8)

To bound the possible positive cap defect, expand it BEFORE estimating:

    d*M_k-C_beta*T
       =d*(M_k-C_beta*s/2)+C_beta*(3-c)/2
                   +(C_beta*d/2)*(1-v_mass)*2^(m-2).

By(3.3),(3.4), s/M_k<=2/C_beta. Therefore

    (Delta_k)_+*s
       <=(2d/C_beta)*K_k+3+d*(1-v_mass)*2^(m-2).            (5.9)

Define the explicit nonnegative error allowance

    A_err,m=c-1+(2d/C_beta)*K_k+3
                                  +d*(1-v_mass)*2^(m-2).

Since Hub_(L_k)(r)<=L_k*(r)_+, the last retained value satisfies

    0<=Phi_k(x(N))
       <=L_k^2+(L_k*sigma_(k-1)/2)*A_err,m.                 (5.10)

All constants c,d,C_beta remain bounded, and sigma_k,sigma_(k-1)
are at most sigma_1 eventually. Formula(3.10) pays (5.10), including
the changed cap ratio and its 2^(m-2) mass correction. Thus
B_(n_m)*Phi_k(x(N))/R_m^2 tends to zero uniformly along the frozen
growing policy. The bound is quantitative and applies to every physical
bridge; it does not require exact absorption of the final component.

## 6. Screen III: why the same comparison does not close the middle

This screen was rejected as a complete uniform comparison. The exact
failure is stronger than merely saying that the middle is coupled.

Fix 2<=i<=k-1 and put L_i=C_beta*sigma_i. At a sample with r_i>L_i,
output isolation gives Q_(i-1)=0. Maximizing over P at fixed Q in
(2.1), r_i>L_i forces the UPPER circular boundary, so

    P_i=sigma_i*p(Q_i/sigma_i),
    L_i-P_i=sigma_i*[sqrt(1+2beta)
               -sqrt((1+beta)^2-(beta+Q_i/sigma_i)^2)].     (6.1)

Unlike at i=k, this cap loss need not be zero. Concavity of p gives

    0<=L_i-P_i<=(C_beta-beta)*Q_i.                          (6.2)

After the last entry into r_i>L_i, let s_entry be its step index.
As long as that terminal excursion persists, its exact signed loss is

    r_i(N)=r_i(s_entry+1)
                   -(L_i/2) sum_(t>s_entry) h_t
                   +D_i^cap(s_entry),
    D_i^cap(s_entry)=(1/2)sum_(t>s_entry)h_t*(L_i-P_i(t)).   (6.3)

This is a tail-specific cap loss, not the all-time source X_m. The
entry is bounded by L_i+b_j*sigma_(i-1)/2. Its following H_j pays
the full horizontal-cap part exactly as in(5.7). However D_i^cap
has not been paid. If Q_i>0, formula(4.4) does give U_(i+1)=0,
but the next coordinate then follows

    x_(i+1)^+=x_(i+1)+(h/2)(Q_i-beta*Q_(i+1)).              (6.4)

Thus bounding the accumulated Q_i in(6.2) requires its actual
downstream negative excursions and the NEXT neighboring gradient.
Deleting beta*Q_(i+1), or assuming a frozen output, is not legitimate.
When Q_i=0, later horizontal motion of component i+1 can recreate a
negative output at a large bridge. No entry-and-persistence statement
for a fixed quadratic core or a fixed face has been proved here.

There are two explicit quantitative failures of a cap-only closure.

First, even the always-full-cap idealization cannot pay the available
incoming horizontal estimate for a middle coordinate. The full
prehistory bound from(2.5) is

    r_i(0)<=sigma_(i-1)*S_pre/2-A_i, i>=2.                 (6.5)

Dropping -A_i and comparing its leading coefficient to L_i*S_(m-1)/2
gives the limiting ratio

    sigma_(i-1)*S_pre/(C_beta*sigma_i*S_(m-1))
                   ->(lambda-1)/(lambda-2)=2+sqrt(2)>1.    (6.6)

The bound in(6.5) is not the actual value, and does not show that an
always-positive horizontal excursion occurs. It shows why one cannot
discard that branch from a last-entry proof without a stronger actual
prehistory estimate. The endpoint proofs avoided this: the first used
its permanent negative input, and the last used its own exact negative
incoming residual. Neither argument applies automatically to a middle
input after all later checkpoints.

Keeping the omitted A_i does not repair this particular comparison:
A_i=sigma_(i-1)*y_(i-1)+sigma_i*y_i/beta, so(3.2),(3.8)
give A_i/(sigma_(i-1)*S_(m-1))->0 uniformly. Thus the positive
leading allowance in(6.6) survives that exact static threshold.

Second, the best state-independent lower bound for P_i on r_i>L_i
from this same circular geometry is its infimum beta*sigma_i. To see
why a larger fixed lower bound is unavailable, fix r>L_i and q in(0,1).
For q sufficiently close to1 put

    t=(r-sigma_i*p(q))/(p(q)-beta)>0,
    w=sigma_i*q+t*(q+beta).

At the centered point(r,-w), the displacement from sigma_i*v(q) is
t*(p(q)-beta,-q-beta), an outward circular normal. Hence the actual
projection for the SAME envelope is sigma_i*v(q). Letting q approach1
gives P_i approaching beta*sigma_i while r remains fixed above L_i.
Other coordinates can be chosen with the permanent x_1 bound intact.
These are alternative states of the unchanged function, NOT states
proved reachable from initialization. Their purpose is only to test a
uniform cap-floor lemma, not to refute trajectory-specific control.

Using that valid floor in a last-entry comparison leaves the positive
coefficient

    d*sigma_(i-1)-beta*sigma_i.

For i=2 it is a fixed positive fraction of sigma_1 eventually, by
(3.8) and beta->0. The entry at level m-2 in the maximum of the
comparison therefore leaves an allowance of order
sigma_1*S_(m-2). Multiplying the horizontal-value allowance by
C_beta*sigma_2 and the original benchmark gives order lambda^(2k),
by(3.6)--(3.9). This comparison does not even give a bounded normalized
upper estimate, let alone one below1-T_infty. This is the scale of a
FAILED UPPER BOUND, not an attained terminal lower bound.

The cap-only method is therefore rejected without pretending that
(6.3) is a closed recursion. A viable replacement must simultaneously
pay the actual incoming middle residuals and the ordered cap losses
D_i^cap, or directly bound the complete actual middle value. Both
neighbors must be retained in that estimate. No universal distance
descent or large-step Markov positivity was assumed: expansion of
distance would retain h*(2-h) times the gradient-square term, with
its negative sign for h>2. The present endpoint proofs instead pay
each large step through its following physical subtree.

## 7. Exact new handoff and limits of the result

Let

    V_mid,m=sum_(i=2)^(k-1) Phi_i(x(N)),
    epsilon_m=(B_(n_m)/R_m^2)*
       [2sigma_1^2+L_k^2+(L_k*sigma_(k-1)/2)*A_err,m].

Every quantity uses the same unchanged static function and its actual
full-prehistory trajectory. The jointly derived estimates prove, at
the UNVERIFIED DRAFT level,

    0<=epsilon_m->0,
    T_m+(B_(n_m)/R_m^2)*V_mid,m
       <=B_(n_m)*2F(x_(n_m))/R_m^2
       <=T_m+(B_(n_m)/R_m^2)*V_mid,m+epsilon_m.             (7.1)

This is a quantitative removal of two terminal contributions from
the comparison, not physical deletion of either component. They
remain in the actual gradient throughout. One CANNOT repeat the
endpoint argument by renaming component2 as component1: U_2 has not
been shown zero, and its incoming Q_1 has not been removed. Similarly,
component k-1 continues to have its original moving output and Q_(k-1).

The remaining actual quantity is V_mid,m for k-2 growing middle
components. No bound below1-T_infty for its normalized limsup is
established, and no normalized lower witness is established. The
defect D_i^cap and the incoming r_i(0) are precise unpaid mechanisms
in the failed third comparison, not a claimed substitute theorem.
We have also NOT decided whether B_(n_m)*X_m/R_m^2 tends to zero.

Accordingly the frozen policy remains OPEN. The original question
over all words and all horizons remains OPEN even if this policy were
eventually decided. The assigned one round ends with the quantitative
endpoint progress and the explicit middle comparison failure; it does
not automatically renew the allocation.

## 8. Retrieval, resources, and persistence

Skills used: query-memory, direct-proving, construct-counterexamples,
search-math-results. The formal gate required by the usual worker
promotion workflow was explicitly disallowed by TASK.md and current
guidance, so every assertion in this report remains unverified.

Full shared evidence84a4435a88a9f487 and a9423b621329882d was read,
as was obstacle03228f06cb53bad3. The first broad recall displays were
truncated; required source evidence was then read in bounded complete
sections. Fact search found no accepted result for this coupled
growing prefix. The statement and relevant proof of04219554e41d9750
were read; no other accepted fact is used.

Retrieval stage: mature_subproblem. Search mode: repair, directed at
last-crossing bounds with neighboring flux and possible schedule
robustness. Matlas returned unrelated projected/noisy-subgradient,
DC, small-step self-contraction and line-search results. Their
hypotheses do not match this fixed long-step trajectory. Web search
located the existing Silver and composition literature, but supplied
no justified transfer to the perturbed word. No external theorem was
imported, and no new technical literature branch was opened. In
particular a smaller effective smoothness was not assumed to make
this word a certified Silver schedule.

Three comparisons were screened: (I) accumulated/signed flux;
(II) state-dependent terminal last entries, fully developed at the
two endpoints; (III) extension to middle components via cap loss,
rejected as a complete cap-only uniform comparison. No fourth
comparison, construction repair or additional checkpoint was used.

Mathematical programs0; numerical evaluations0; arithmetic replays0;
executed recurrences0; solvers0; benchmark DP0; formal submissions0;
new accepted facts0; installations0; consults0; subagents0.
Only administrative text reads/writes, memory operations, retrieval,
and file-integrity metadata checks were performed. All old grants
remain closed. New files are confined to the assigned absolute public
directory actual_retired_flux_20261001; existing private memory is
appended for continuation. No truth-store files were edited directly.

Expected completed count after return61; the official post-return
counter has not been observed. Completed ceiling61 / attempted62.
No automatic renewal, publication, finalization, revocation, shutdown,
or original-Goal completion is performed. No work remains in flight.

# Frozen discovery specification, round 62

This is numerical discovery, with no fact submission. Source findings
84a4435a88a9f487 and d5345c6e59f22ce3 were read in full. Their mathematical
status remains joint unverified. No asymptotic theorem is asserted at the
finite depths below. All old allocations remain closed.

## Static function and exact formulas, before execution

For each enclosing m in {16,20,25}, set k=floor(sqrt(m)), J=m-k,
beta=1/k, lambda=1+sqrt(2), eta_word=1-1/(4m),
c=lambda*(1+1/(4m)), a=eta_word/c. Every child word uses THESE parameters.
The physical step at zero-based index t is b_v2(t+1), with
b_j=1+a*c^j; H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j). The length is n=2^m-1.
The selected indices are 2^(J+i)-1 for i=0,...,k-1. The ordinary block
levels are J,J,J+1,...,m-2. Their masses obey S_0=0,
S_(j+1)=2S_j+b_j. The final suffix starts at query 2^(m-1).

Write C=beta+sqrt(1+2beta), R_circle=1+beta, kap=1+beta^2.
In coordinates (p,-q), the FULL convex body is
K={0<=q<=1, beta*q<=p, (p-beta)^2+(q+beta)^2<=R_circle^2}.
Its boundary consists of the segment from 0 to (C,0), the segment
from 0 to (beta,-1), and the circular arc connecting their far endpoints.
The displayed disk intersects those two half-planes in exactly the
horizontal sections beta*q<=p<=beta+sqrt(R_circle^2-(q+beta)^2).

For any centered normalized input w=(r,s), its projection is w if
w belongs to K. Otherwise a nearest boundary point is among:
(clip(r,0,C),0); (beta*t,-t), t=clip((beta*r-s)/kap,0,1);
and the radial point (beta,beta)+R_circle*(w-(beta,beta))/||w-(beta,beta)||
when that point lies on the stated arc. These are the exact nearest
points on the two segments and on the arc whenever the latter has an
interior stationary minimum; remaining arc minima are endpoints already
included. Choose the candidate maximizing <g,w>-||g||^2/2.
The production implementation can return the radial disk projection
directly when it belongs to the arc, since K is a subset of that disk.
For s>=0 the horizontal projection is exact. For r<=0 the diagonal
projection is exact, by maximizing first over U=p-beta*q>=0.
These are projection calculations, never projections of the GD state.

The full support function at d=(d_x,d_y) is the maximum of
0, C*d_x, beta*d_x-d_y, and
beta*(d_x+d_y)+R_circle*sqrt(d_x^2+d_y^2) if d_x>0, d_y<0 and
(-d_y)*sqrt(1+2beta)>=beta*d_x. This is obtained by maximizing the
linear functional along the entire arc; the derivative has a unique
interior maximum in precisely that angular sector. The endpoints pay
all other directions, including d_x=0. Thus projection KKT is checked
against the whole convex hull by h_K(w-g)-<w-g,g>, not sampled arc points.

For an ordinary block of level j, construct its local data backwards
from u=beta,y=1. Reading the actual reversed physical step h, alpha=h/2,
let z=max(beta/sqrt(1+2beta),(y+(1-alpha)*beta)/(u-(1-alpha)*beta)).
If z takes the first branch use q=0,p=C; otherwise use
q=(1+beta)*z/sqrt(1+z^2)-beta, p=beta+(1+beta)/sqrt(1+z^2).
Then u<-u+alpha*p, y<-y+alpha*q. These are exactly the frozen source's
backward rule, with no asymptotic M approximation.

The sole permitted aggregation is the invariant capped backward prefix:
once q=0, y<=beta*(u-C)/sqrt(1+2beta). Further backward steps increase u
by h*C/2 and keep y unchanged, preserving that inequality. A remaining
prefix of r steps has exactly floor(r/2^l)-floor(r/2^(l+1)) occurrences
of level l. Its EXACT mass is the sum of these counts times b_l.
Only this already derived invariant prefix can be aggregated. Save every
count and the mass used; distinguish explicit backward steps from these
implicit steps. No forward update may be aggregated.

For component i=1,...,k, let u_i,y_i be these backward data and
M_i=u_i+y_i/beta. Start ell_1=0,D_1=1 and set
sigma_i=D_i/M_i, ell_(i+1)=sigma_i*y_i,
A_i=ell_i+ell_(i+1)/beta,
D_(i+1)=sigma_i*(b_(J+i-1)/2-1).
Set delta=D_(k+1)/(1+S_(m-1)). The one frozen function is
F(x)=(1/2)*sum_i E_(sigma_i*K)(x_i-A_i,x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1))
     +(1/2)*Hub_delta(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1)), starting at e_1.
Here E_C(w)=max_(g in C)(<g,w>-||g||^2/2), and Hub_delta is
0 for t<=0, t^2/2 for 0<t<delta, and delta*t-delta^2/2 above delta.
All half factors are physical. Projection scaling gives
(P_i,-Q_i)=sigma_i*Pi_K((x_i-A_i)/sigma_i,
                       (x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1))/sigma_i).
Let P_(k+1)=clip(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1),0,delta), Q_0=0.
The entire gradient is (P_i-Q_(i-1))/2, including all old/future terms.
Every actual step is x<-x-h*gradient. No state clipping, checkpoint
replacement, independent suffix initialization, or face replay is allowed.

Completing the square in E proves its gradient is its Euclidean
projection. Projection optimality gives the firmly nonexpansive inequality,
so E is convex and 1-smooth. Stacking adjacent pairs and the final scalar
counts coordinates at most twice; the half-sum F is convex and 1-smooth.
Its minimum set is x_i<=A_i, x_(i+1)>=beta*(x_i-ell_i), and
x_(k+1)<=ell_(k+1). The point x_star=(A_1,ell_2,0,...,0) is feasible.
Since 1-A_1=sigma_1*u_1, ell_2=beta*A_1=sigma_1*y_1, and
u_1>=beta*y_1, we have A_1<=1/(1+beta^2). For x_1<0 the initial
distance is at least 1; on 0<=x_1<=A_1 its square is bounded below
by (1-x_1)^2+beta^2*x_1^2, decreasing on this interval and attained
by x_star. Therefore the TRUE radius, used in every display, is
R_m^2=(1-A_1)^2+ell_2^2=(u_1^2+y_1^2)/M_1^2.

The actual terminal numerator 2F is computed from every component.
Independently compare the Huber term with D_(k+1)^2/[2(1+S_(m-1))].
During the ACTUAL last suffix accumulate, without clipping U_i=P_i-beta*Q_i,
X_flux=(1/2)*sum_t h_t sum_(i<k) sigma_i*(theta*U_i+U_(i+1)),
theta=(1-beta)/(1+beta), and at the terminal add
X_terminal=C*sum_i sigma_i*max(x_i-A_i,0).
The positive part here is the definition of X, not a change to the state.

## Six semantic slots, frozen before execution

1. Calibration, including construction of m16 constants. Exactly four
   local normalized cases at beta=1/4, sigma=1:
   (a) w=(0,-17/32), projection=(1/8,-1/2), envelope=17/128;
   (b) w=(1/4,-1/4), projection=w, envelope=1/16;
   (c) w=(2C,0), projection=(C,0), envelope=3C^2/2;
   (d) w=(9/4,-5/4), projection=(5/4,-1/2), envelope=81/32.
   One short whole-function trajectory: the first TWO m16 updates,
   with h=b_0,b_1. Preregistered exact coordinates are
   x_t=(1-C*sigma_1*sum_(s<t)h_s/2,0,...,0), t=0,1,2.
   At each of these queries the symbolic cap hypothesis is
   u_1-C*sum_(s<t)h_s/2>=C+sqrt(1+2beta)*y_1/beta.
   It must be checked and must pass, otherwise calibration fails.
   Gradient=C*sigma_1*e_1/2;
   F=(C*sigma_1*(x_1-A_1)-(C*sigma_1)^2/2)/2.
   Check x_star, its zero objective/gradient, minimum-set feasibility,
   and both true-radius formulas. No compiler is installed; source
   identity and Python runtime data replace an executable compiler manifest.
2. Full binary64 m16, k=4, n=65535. Save EVERY query 0 through n,
   including coordinates, full gradient, projections, component objectives,
   and full objective. All 65535 updates must be executed.
3. Full binary64 m20, k=4, n=1048575. Save stride 16 queries (65536
   ordinary samples), plus all selected pre/post checkpoints and terminal.
4. Full binary64 m25, k=5, n=33554431. Save stride 512 queries (65536
   ordinary samples), plus the same special queries. This slot may be
   retired if the installed runtime cannot support it in the fixed cap.
5. Independently evolve m16 using standard-library Decimal at precision
   65 significant decimal digits, round-half-even. Recompute sqrt(2), all
   word parameters, backward data, offsets and projections in that context.
   Compare EVERY query against the saved binary64 fields. Binary states
   and faces never drive this evolution. Save all aggregate errors plus
   stride 1024 decimal samples, selected checkpoints, terminal and bounded
   error extremizers. Full decimal states are NOT archived.
6. Independently coded projection/gradient/objective/radius check of ALL
   SAVED ordinary and special samples from the largest completed case.
   It uses the explicit candidate maximization formula rather than the
   production projection's region shortcuts. It performs no new trajectory.
   Alternatively this slot is the sole correction of one failed job, with
   the exact source change recorded; it cannot also perform readback.

Each job reserves 50 accounted seconds BEFORE numerical imports or model
construction, and is externally killed after 49.5 seconds to remain within
the 50-second whole-job cap. No numerical code runs outside these slots.
Failure and timeout consume their slot. No seventh job or automatic retry.
After failed calibration only the sole correction is available; if that
does not succeed, retire all unused slots and close.

All diagnostics run INSIDE the corresponding charged process. They count
every projection, update, local backward update, and diagnostic query.
Full query coverage of feasibility and KKT includes terminal queries.
Save component face counts and transitions, last activity, U minima,
positive input excursions and their largest values, last cap excursions,
first/middle/last terminal contributions, X, true radius and Huber comparison.
Feasibility and normalized KKT failure tolerance is 5e-12 for binary64
and 1e-55 for Decimal. No mathematical conclusion is certified by this
tolerance. Save every failure; stop with an incomplete run if 10000 failure
records are reached, keeping all recorded failures. No clipping repairs.
At most 4+2k diagnostic extremizer states are retained in a separate archive.
Larger binary archives are explicitly SAMPLED, never a full replay claim.
Output is limited to 128 MiB per job; fixed binary records keep ordinary
archives well below that cap. A bounded progress record permits honest
partial-count reporting on timeout.

For benchmark displays only the accepted saved table's b=1,j=11 endpoints
are used. Its SHA256 is
2a7585bc0bade9f85461456c13d60bbe618f85c7cc816e1ddc5b4c1a1d611e68.
With D80=1208925819614629174706176,
L2047=46381188529515973069682150122,
H2048=46409982540259750875314341333, and m>=11,
lambda^(m-11)*L2047/D80 <= B_(2^m-1)
 <= lambda^(m-11)*(sqrt(H2048/D80)+3/100)^2.
The lower direction follows from doubling monotonicity; the upper from
the accepted uniform ray enclosure. The hypotheses use the actual physical
index 2^m-1. Floating displays are NOT certified intervals. No new DP,
asymptotic finite-depth assertion, exact lower witness, or upper proof.

On closure only stored-output reading, symbolic interpretation, hashing and
administrative reporting are allowed. Unused credits are retired.

# Same circular function: terminal values, positive-charge overcount, and precision failure

Status: NUMERICAL DISCOVERY ONLY. No fact was submitted or accepted. The
original all-word/all-horizon problem and the asymptotic middle-component
question remain OPEN. The one allocated max3 round after baseline61 is
finished; expected completed count62, ceiling62/attempted63. The official
post-return counter has not been observed. No automatic renewal.

The most useful observation is at m16 with independently recomputed
65-significant-digit decimal arithmetic: all four circular terminal values
were numerically zero, while the original accumulated X_m was positive at
the original benchmark scale. The full terminal objective was carried by
the terminal Huber and agreed with its source formula to relative error
3.8445684035925723e-44. This is finite-depth evidence that X_m can count
activity later dissipated. It is not an exact zero, a certified lower or
upper bound, or an asymptotic conclusion.

A second essential result is a numerical reliability failure. The complete
binary64 m16 and m20 histories both ended at a numerically zero objective,
and both had relative error 1 against their positive terminal Huber formula.
The independent m16 decimal evolution differed from binary64 by as much as
0.1244610627244608 in one coordinate. Local projection KKT checks passed
throughout both evolutions. Thus small local residuals do not certify the
trajectory's accuracy through these large steps.

## What was frozen and executed

The full evidence of source findings84a4435a88a9f487 and d5345c6e59f22ce3
was read. These remain joint unverified drafts, not accepted predecessors.
The construction was preserved exactly as a mathematical specification:
k=floor(sqrt(m)), beta_arc=1/k, eta_word=1-1/(4m),
c=(1+sqrt(2))*(1+1/(4m)), a=eta_word/c; the first occurrences of levels
m-k,...,m-1 in the same enclosing-m physical ruler word; the full circular
convex body; all offsets from the exact local backward rule; the static
half-sum of adjacent circular envelopes and terminal half-Huber; x_0=e_1.
All actual old and future component gradients remained in every update.

The pre-execution derivation and all six semantic slots are in
preregistration.md, appended verbatim to the complete shared evidence.
They include the circle's center and radius, exact Euclidean projection,
full-circle support function, global gradient and half factors, backward
rule, true closest radius, exact short calibration, diagnostics, raw archive
formats, benchmark directions and resource limits. No finite-plane
surrogate, extra component, state clipping, independent checkpoint reset,
mass-only forward replacement, or unallocated mathematical case was used.

Source discovery.py SHA256:
e4b4084cf8741418dbb89e772682047f82f9508d800b96d04f274397c61b05df.
Controller run_reserved_job.py SHA256:
aef12f0500b4cbf4b25eee3cbb660a1d2c2cfde4918fe744ad59646214dcce4d.
All frozen hashes were reread and matched after closure. No code correction
was used. Python3.12.14 was installed; no compiler or NumPy/mpmath/gmpy2
runtime was available, and nothing was installed.

The local backward construction aggregates only the invariant q=0 prefix.
At q=0 its normal-cone inequality persists as u increases by h*C_beta/2
and y stays fixed. Remaining ruler-level counts and the complete skipped
mass were saved per block. This replaces no forward update.

## Charged slots and exact coverage

| Slot | Frozen purpose | Outcome | External measured seconds |
|---|---|---|---:|
| 1 | Four exact local cases and the first two whole-function m16 updates | Completed; calibration passed | 0.06833370299136732 |
| 2 | Complete binary64 m16 | Completed | 1.6586024150019512 |
| 3 | Complete binary64 m20 | Completed | 15.561947152993525 |
| 4 | Complete binary64 m25 | Retired without execution: installed runtime unsupported within cap | 0 |
| 5 | Independent 65-digit m16 and comparison at every query | Completed | 4.579721163012437 |
| 6 | Independent saved-sample m20 projection/gradient/objective/radius check | Completed | 1.6722831789957127 |

Slots were executed in order1,2,5,3,6; all six definitions were frozen
before slot1. Slot5 was brought forward after the stored binary64 Huber
discrepancy, without changing its content. Each launched process reserved
50 accounted seconds BEFORE numerical imports or construction. An external
49.5-second kill cap kept the entire job below the allowed50 seconds.
Imports, construction, all diagnostics and raw export were inside that cap.

The m20 job already required15.56 seconds for1048575 updates and four
components. The frozen m25 slot requires33554431 updates and five
components. With no compiled implementation available, the unchanged full
case was unsupported in50 seconds. The expressly allowed retirement was
used; there was no partial m25 trajectory, parameter substitution, word
shortening, installation, additional precision case, or replacement job.

Counters are committed to a memory-mapped file after each operation and
also saved in completed results. Here every launched job completed normally,
so there is no truncated trajectory or counter uncertainty:
